<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the energy part of the generalized ionization conjecture for full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states. The energy needed to remove <i>m</i> electrons is asymptotic to $`a_{\mathrm{TF}}m^{7/3}`$ whenever $`m\to\infty`$ and $`Z/m\to\infty`$, with the Thomas–Fermi constant in atomic units. We also obtain both conjectured iterated limits.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states, $$\label{eq:joint-limit} \frac{I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}}\longrightarrow a_{\mathrm{TF}}>0 \quad\text{whenever}\quad m\longrightarrow\infty, \qquad \frac Zm\longrightarrow\infty,$$ where $Z>m\ge1$ are integers. In particular, $$\begin{aligned} \lim_{m\to\infty} \frac{\limsup_{Z\to\infty} I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}} &=a_{\mathrm{TF}},\label{eq:iterated-upper}\\ \lim_{m\to\infty} \frac{\liminf_{Z\to\infty} I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}} &=a_{\mathrm{TF}}.\label{eq:iterated-lower} \end{aligned}$$ The inner limits are taken over integer $Z$ with $m$ fixed. No convergence of $I_m(Z)$ at a fixed $m$ is asserted or assumed.
  - Explanation: The normalized ionization energy converges to the positive Thomas–Fermi coefficient in the joint regime; the iterated upper and lower limits follow without fixed removed-electron-number convergence.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-priced-deficit)): There is a universal number $q>0$ such that, for every sequence satisfying $$\lambda=s^{-4},\qquad s\longrightarrow0, \qquad Zs^3\longrightarrow\infty,$$ and every choice of $n$ minimizing $E_n+\lambda n$, one has $$s^3(Z-n)\longrightarrow q.$$ The number $q$ is the exterior Coulomb charge of the unique classical solution, tending uniformly to zero at infinity, of $$\Delta F=4\pi k\bigl((F-1)_+\bigr)^{3/2} \quad\text{on }\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\}, \qquad k=(5T/3)^{-3/2},$$ that is bounded above and below by positive multiples of $\|x\|^{-4}$ near zero. The density $k((F-1)_+)^{3/2}$ has bounded support, and $F(x)=q/\|x\|$ outside that support. Moreover, $$a_{\mathrm{TF}}=\frac37q^{-4/3}.$$
  - Explanation: Every priced minimizing sector has the same asymptotic deficit. Its coefficient is the exterior charge of the unique decaying singular profile and determines the ionization coefficient.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-inputs)): The sequence $E_n$ is nonincreasing. If $E_n<E_{n-1}$, there is a normalized ground state in sector $n$, and $n\le2Z+1\le3Z$. There is a largest such index $N_*$, with $Z\le N_*\le3Z$, and $$E=E_{N_*}=E_n\quad(n\ge N_*).$$ There are universal $C,Z_0$ such that $$\label{eq:neutral-offset} E_Z(Z)\le E+C\qquad(Z\ge Z_0).$$ If $\Psi$ is a normalized sector ground state and $F$ is a bounded real symmetric Lipschitz function of the spatial configuration, then $$\label{eq:ground-multiplier} \langle F\Psi,H_{Z,n}F\Psi\rangle -E_n\\|F\Psi\\|_2^2 =\frac12\mathbb E_\Psi\|\nabla F\|^2,$$ where the left side is interpreted as a quadratic form and the gradient includes all spatial coordinates. Finally, every unshifted $\delta$-state in a sector $n\le3Z$ satisfies $$\label{eq:global-density-input} \int_{\mathbb R^3}\rho_\psi^{5/3}\le C(Z^{7/3}+\delta).$$
  - Explanation: Monotonicity, strict binding, a neutral energy offset, and the multiplier identity supply sector and density inputs.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-field-input)): For every normalized unshifted $\delta$-state of finite particle number, $P_*\le C$. Fix $y\ne0$ and $C_0\ge1$. The data $X$ may be trivial. Otherwise, suppose that the initial cut, or finite sequence of cuts $(o_h,c_h)$, has total out support covered by at most $C_0$ cells $B_z$ with $$C_0^{-1}\le a_z/a_y\le C_0, \qquad \sum_h\bigl(\|\nabla o_h\|^2+\|\nabla c_h\|^2\bigr) \le\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\mathbf 1_{B_z}, \qquad \frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\le C_0\sqrt{P_*}.$$ Then, for the complete cut data, $$\label{eq:local-field-input} \left\\|(\mathbb E[J_y\mid X])_+\right\\|_2 \le C(C_0)\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ The constants are independent of $Z$, the sector, the state, $y$, and $\delta$, apart from the displayed dependence through $m_y$.
  - Explanation: Local screened fields are controlled for arbitrary finite energy offsets under the stated cut hypotheses.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-screening-inputs)): Let $\psi$ be a normalized unshifted $\delta$-state of finite particle number. For fixed $0<\alpha<\beta<\infty$ and every $u>0$, $$\label{eq:annular-count} \left\\|\#\{i:\alpha u\le\|x_i\|\le\beta u\}\right\\|_2 \le C_{\alpha,\beta} \bigl(\max(u^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta u}\bigr).$$ Suppose in addition that $n\le3Z$, and set $$B_{u/8}(x)=V(x)-\sum_{\|x_i\|<u/8}\frac1{\|x-x_i\|}.$$ Let $Y$ be trivial or be the data of a square cut whose out support lies in $\{u/4\le\|x\|\le8u\}$ and whose squared gradient sum is at most $C/u^2$. There are conditional versions harmonic on $u/4<\|x\|<6u$ for which $$\label{eq:conditional-inner-field} \left\\|\sup_{u/2\le\|x\|\le4u} \bigl(\mathbb E[B_{u/8}(x)\mid Y]\bigr)_+\right\\|_2 \le C\left(\frac{\max(u^{-3},1)}u+\sqrt{\frac\delta u}\right).$$ The constant in the second estimate may depend on the fixed gradient bound. Both estimates allow every finite $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Annular particle counts and conditional inner fields admit offset-dependent bounds; the field estimate requires the stated sector and cut restrictions.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-priced-rough)): The minimum $P(\lambda)$ is attained. Along <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, every minimizing index $n$ is eventually nonzero and strictly bound. For a normalized ground state $\Psi$ in that sector, put $D_0=E_n-E$. Uniformly over all these choices, $$\label{eq:rough-deficit} \|Z-n\|\le Cs^{-3},\qquad 0\le D_0\le Cs^{-7}.$$
  - Explanation: The priced minimum is attained and eventually selects a nonzero strictly bound sector with controlled deficit and energy offset.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff)): There is a sufficiently large fixed $R_0$ such that, for every sequence of minimizing sectors and corresponding ground states in <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, $$\label{eq:exterior-cutoff} s^3\mathbb E_\Psi\#\{i:\|x_i\|>R_0s\}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The rescaled expected electron count outside a sufficiently large fixed exterior scale vanishes.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-event-tilt)): Let $j<J_*$ and let $A'\in\mathcal F_j$ have probability $p>0$. There is a normalized antisymmetric state $\Psi_{A'}$ in the same sector whose raw law is the raw marginal conditioned on $A'$ and whose form energy satisfies $$\langle\Psi_{A'},H_{Z,n}\Psi_{A'}\rangle \leq E_n+C r_j^{-2.02}\log^5(e/p). \label{eq:event-tilt-cost}$$ The constant is independent of the particle number, the number of observed arrays, and $D_0$.
  - Explanation: Conditioning on an observation event can be realized by a state in the same sector with a controlled energy cost.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-inverse-comparison)): There is a universal constant $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$ with the following property. Fix $$M\geq2,\qquad 0<h_l<h_h<\infty,\qquad 0<\xi<h_l/16.$$ For all sufficiently far systems along <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, at every $j<J_*$ there is an event $\mathcal G_j\in\mathcal F_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\mathbb P(\mathcal G_j^c)\leq Cr^{25}.$$ On $\mathcal G_j$, the following assertions hold simultaneously for $r\leq d_y\leq4Mr$ and $h_l\leq h\leq h_h$: $$\begin{split} d_y^4H'_j(y)&\leq C_{\mathrm{cap}},\\ d_y^6\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\geq h-\xi,\\ d_y^6\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\leq h+\xi. \end{split} \label{eq:inverse-comparison}$$ The probability constant and the threshold for sufficiently far systems may depend on the displayed fixed parameters and on the fixed packet conventions; $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$ is independent of the band and accuracy parameters.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous inverse density–field implications hold on a high-probability event, with a universal upper field cap and parameter-dependent probability and threshold constants.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-barrier-invariant)): There are fixed positive constants $\varepsilon,B,C_{\mathrm{inv}}$ and $M>2$ with the following property. For every sufficiently far system in <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, use the observations and kernels of [eq:observation-scales,eq:master-kernel] [eq:observation-scales](#eq:observation-scales), [eq:master-kernel](#eq:master-kernel) with this $\varepsilon$. For every $0\le j\le J_*$ there exist $\mathcal F_j$-measurable functions $u_j,p_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\label{eq:barrier-invariant} \begin{aligned} \Delta u_j&\ge-4\pi\nu +4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{d<r\}}\mu_j-4\pi p_j +\mathbf 1_{\{d\ge r\}}f(u_j),\\ b_r\le u_j&\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\quad(d\ge r),\\ u_j&=b_r\quad(d>Mr). \end{aligned}$$ The first inequality holds in distributions on $\mathbb R^3$. For each fixed system, $u_j-V$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz, with deterministic bounds on compact sets; $p_j$ is a nonnegative bounded density supported in $\{d\le r_j\}$, also with a deterministic bound. Its expected total mass satisfies $$\label{eq:barrier-error-mass} \sup_{0\le j\le J_*}\mathbb E\int p_j \le C\bigl(r_0^{32}+s^{19/2-3w}\bigr)=o(1).$$ The deterministic fixed-system regularity bounds need not be uniform in $Z$; the constants in <a href="#eq:barrier-error-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:barrier-error-mass">[eq:barrier-error-mass]</a> are uniform.
  - Explanation: A conditional lower barrier propagates through observation scales while retaining the nuclear source and controlling the expected error mass.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-singular-profile)): There is at most one classical solution $F$ of $$\Delta F=4\pi k\bigl((F-1)_+\bigr)^{3/2} \quad\text{on }\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\}$$ which tends uniformly to zero at infinity and satisfies $c\|x\|^{-4}\le F(x)\le C\|x\|^{-4}$ near zero for some $c,C>0$. Every such solution has the uniform singular asymptotic $$\label{eq:sommerfeld-coefficient} \|x\|^4F(x)\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm S}:=\left(\frac{12}{4\pi k}\right)^2 \qquad(\|x\|\downarrow0).$$ If a solution exists, it is radial.
  - Explanation: A decaying classical profile with a positive singularity is unique, has the Sommerfeld coefficient, and is radial if it exists.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-sector-limit)): Let $q$ be the deficit coefficient in <a href="#thm:priced-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:priced-deficit">2</a>. For every sequence of integers $Z>m\ge1$ such that $m\to\infty$ and $Z/m\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:fixed-sector-limit} \frac{E_Z(Z-m)-E_Z(Z)}{m^{7/3}} \longrightarrow\frac37q^{-4/3}.$$
  - Explanation: Integrating priced deficits and bracketing sectors recovers the joint fixed-sector ionization asymptotic without sector-energy convexity.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tf-identification)): With the two-spin coefficient $T$ fixed above, $$\label{eq:tf-unit-deficit} \lim_{Z\to\infty} \bigl[E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z-1)-E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)\bigr] =\frac37q^{-4/3}.$$ The limit holds through real charges $Z>1$. Consequently <a href="#eq:tf-constant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:tf-constant">[eq:tf-constant]</a> holds for every fixed real $m>0$, with $$a_{\mathrm{TF}}=\frac37q^{-4/3}>0.$$
  - Explanation: The Thomas–Fermi unit deficit identifies the quantum coefficient, and exact mass scaling extends the classical law to fixed positive real removed masses.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-screening-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\psi$ be a normalized unshifted $\\delta$-state of finite particle number. For fixed $0<\\alpha<\\beta<\\infty$ and every $u>0$, $$\\label{eq:annular-count}\n \\left\\|\\#\\{i:\\alpha u\\le|x_i|\\le\\beta u\\}\\right\\|_2\n   \\le C_{\\alpha,\\beta}\n           \\bigl(\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}\\bigr).$$ Suppose in addition that $n\\le3Z$, and set $$B_{u/8}(x)=V(x)-\\sum_{|x_i|<u/8}\\frac1{|x-x_i|}.$$ Let $Y$ be trivial or be the data of a square cut whose out support lies in $\\{u/4\\le|x|\\le8u\\}$ and whose squared gradient sum is at most $C/u^2$. There are conditional versions harmonic on $u/4<|x|<6u$ for which $$\\label{eq:conditional-inner-field}\n \\left\\|\\sup_{u/2\\le|x|\\le4u}\n              \\bigl(\\mathbb E[B_{u/8}(x)\\mid Y]\\bigr)_+\\right\\|_2\n \\le C\\left(\\frac{\\max(u^{-3},1)}u+\\sqrt{\\frac\\delta u}\\right).$$ The constant in the second estimate may depend on the fixed gradient bound. Both estimates allow every finite $\\delta$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-priced-rough",
        "exact_quote": "The minimum $P(\\lambda)$ is attained.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-event-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $j<J_*$ and let $A'\\in\\mathcal F_j$ have probability $p>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-inverse-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C_{\\mathrm{cap}}$ with the following property. Fix $$M\\geq2,\\qquad 0<h_l<h_h<\\infty,\\qquad 0<\\xi<h_l/16.$$ For all sufficiently far systems along <a href=\"#eq:scaling-regime\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:scaling-regime\">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, at every $j<J_*$ there is an event $\\mathcal G_j\\in\\mathcal F_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G_j^c)\\leq Cr^{25}.$$ On $\\mathcal G_j$, the following assertions hold simultaneously for $r\\leq d_y\\leq4Mr$ and $h_l\\leq h\\leq h_h$: $$\\begin{split}\n d_y^4H'_j(y)&\\leq C_{\\mathrm{cap}},\\\\\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\\geq h-\\xi,\\\\\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\\leq h+\\xi.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:inverse-comparison}$$ The probability constant and the threshold for sufficiently far systems may depend on the displayed fixed parameters and on the fixed packet conventions; $C_{\\mathrm{cap}}$ is independent of the band and accuracy parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-inverse-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C_{\\mathrm{cap}}$ with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-263.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a universal constant $C_{\\mathrm{cap}}$ with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-barrier-invariant",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\mathrm{inv}}$ and $M>2$ with the following property. For every sufficiently far system in <a href=\"#eq:scaling-regime\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:scaling-regime\">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, use the observations and kernels of [eq:observation-scales,eq:master-kernel] [eq:observation-scales](#eq:observation-scales), [eq:master-kernel](#eq:master-kernel) with this $\\varepsilon$. For every $0\\le j\\le J_*$ there exist $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable functions $u_j,p_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\\label{eq:barrier-invariant}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu\n       +4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j-4\\pi p_j\n       +\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\\\\\n u_j&=b_r\\quad(d>Mr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The first inequality holds in distributions on $\\mathbb R^3$. For each fixed system, $u_j-V$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz, with deterministic bounds on compact sets; $p_j$ is a nonnegative bounded density supported in $\\{d\\le r_j\\}$, also with a deterministic bound. Its expected total mass satisfies $$\\label{eq:barrier-error-mass}\n \\sup_{0\\le j\\le J_*}\\mathbb E\\int p_j\n \\le C\\bigl(r_0^{32}+s^{19/2-3w}\\bigr)=o(1).$$ The deterministic fixed-system regularity bounds need not be uniform in $Z$; the constants in <a href=\"#eq:barrier-error-mass\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:barrier-error-mass\">[eq:barrier-error-mass]</a> are uniform.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-barrier-invariant",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\mathrm{inv}}$ and $M>2$ with the following property. For every $0\\le j\\le J_*$ there exist $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable functions $u_j,p_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\\label{eq:barrier-invariant}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu\n       +4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j-4\\pi p_j\n       +\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\\\\\n u_j&=b_r\\quad(d>Mr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The first inequality holds in distributions on $\\mathbb R^3$. For each fixed system, $u_j-V$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz, with deterministic bounds on compact sets; $p_j$ is a nonnegative bounded density supported in $\\{d\\le r_j\\}$, also with a deterministic bound. Its expected total mass satisfies $$\\label{eq:barrier-error-mass}\n \\sup_{0\\le j\\le J_*}\\mathbb E\\int p_j\n \\le C\\bigl(r_0^{32}+s^{19/2-3w}\\bigr)=o(1).$$ The deterministic fixed-system regularity bounds need not be uniform in $Z$; the constants in <a href=\"#eq:barrier-error-mass\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:barrier-error-mass\">[eq:barrier-error-mass]</a> are uniform.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are fixed positive constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\mathrm{inv}}$ and $M>2$ with the following property. For every sufficiently far system in <a href=\"#eq:scaling-regime\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"eq:scaling-regime\">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, use the observations and kernels of [eq:observation-scales,eq:master-kernel] [eq:observation-scales](#eq:observation-scales), [eq:master-kernel](#eq:master-kernel) with this $\\varepsilon$. For every $0\\le j\\le J_*$ there exist $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable functions $u_j,p_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\\label{eq:barrier-invariant}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu\n       +4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j-4\\pi p_j\n       +\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\\\\\n u_j&=b_r\\quad(d>Mr).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The first inequality holds in distributions on $\\mathbb R^3$. For each fixed system, $u_j-V$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz, with deterministic bounds on compact sets; $p_j$ is a nonnegative bounded density supported in $\\{d\\le r_j\\}$, also with a deterministic bound. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Coulomb Hamiltonian** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): In atomic units, let $Z$ be a positive integer and let $$\label{eq:hamiltonian} H_{Z,N} =\sum_{i=1}^N\left(-\frac12\Delta_i-\frac{Z}{\|x_i\|}\right) +\sum_{1\le i<l\le N}\frac1{\|x_i-x_l\|}$$ act on $\bigwedge^N L^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb C^2)$.
- **Sector ground energy** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): Write $E_Z(N)=\inf\sigma(H_{Z,N})$ and $E_Z(0)=0$.
- **Ionization energy** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): For integers $Z>m\ge1$, define $$I_m(Z)=E_Z(Z-m)-E_Z(Z).$$
- **Thomas–Fermi kinetic coefficient** ([source](#eq:tf-constant)): The kinetic coefficient of the corresponding Thomas–Fermi functional is $T=\frac3{10}(3\pi^2)^{2/3}$.
- **Thomas–Fermi functional** ([source](#eq:tf-constant)): More explicitly, put $$\mathcal T_Z(\rho) =T\int_{\mathbb R^3}\rho^{5/3} -Z\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{\rho(x)}{\|x\|}\,dx +\frac12\iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3} \frac{\rho(x)\rho(y)}{\|x-y\|}\,dx\,dy,$$ and let $E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(N)$ be its infimum over nonnegative densities of mass $N$ and finite displayed terms.
- **Thomas–Fermi ionization energy** ([source](#eq:tf-constant)): Set $I_m^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)=E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z-m)-E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)$.
- **Unconstrained energy and priced minimum** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For fixed $Z$, abbreviate $E_n=E_Z(n)$, and introduce the unconstrained energy and the priced minimum $$E=\inf_{n\ge0}E_n,\qquad P(\lambda)=\min_{n\ge0}\bigl(E_n+\lambda n\bigr),\qquad\lambda>0.$$
- **Unshifted offset state** ([source](#sec:pricing)): If $\psi$ is a normalized finite-sector form-domain vector, we call it an *unshifted $\delta$-state* when its form energy is at most $E+\delta$, where $\delta\ge0$.
- **Positive and negative parts** ([source](#sec:pricing)): For a real number $v$, write $v_+=\max(v,0)$ and $v_-=\max(-v,0)$.
- **Admissible Thomas–Fermi densities** ([source](#pm-h-35)): For clarity, the admissible finite-mass class can be written as $$\mathcal X=\{\rho\ge0:\rho\in L^1(\mathbb R^3)\cap L^{5/3}(\mathbb R^3)\}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-priced-rough) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-priced-rough): Compare the priced objective with neighboring and neutral sectors, then apply offset-dependent screening. -> Attainment, strict binding, and rough deficit and offset bounds.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff): Test the sector eigen-equation on shells and bias the law by shell weight; sum the decaying shell bounds. -> Vanishing rescaled exterior electron count.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-inverse-comparison) / [proof](#sec:comparison): Combine noisy observations, a fresh spatial cut, local energy comparisons, and Coulomb duality; transfer expectations between the separate posterior laws. -> Simultaneous inverse density–field comparisons.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-barrier-invariant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-barrier-invariant): Retain branches on open neighborhoods, apply the weak maximum rule, then average while recording exceptional data as an error density. -> A propagated conditional lower barrier with vanishing expected error mass.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-thm-priced-deficit) / [proof](#sec:limit): Select conditional data, rescale, use local compactness and exterior screening, transfer the positive barrier, and identify the unique profile. -> Every priced minimizing sector has the same positive limiting deficit.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-sector-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-sector-limit): Bound secant slopes by minimizing sectors, integrate through fixed partitions, control the zero-price endpoint, and bracket the desired sector by monotonicity. -> The fixed-sector ionization coefficient in the joint regime.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-tf-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tf-identification): Identify the classical unit-price charge using uniqueness, integrate other prices, and apply exact density scaling. -> Identification with the classical Thomas–Fermi coefficient.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tf-identification-2): Use the joint theorem and a sequential contradiction argument to obtain the specified iterated upper and lower limits. -> The joint and iterated energy limits without fixed removed-electron-number convergence.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I_joint**: The normalized ionization energy approaches the Thomas–Fermi constant only under the stated joint regime.
- **I_minimizers**: The priced charge-deficit limit applies to every minimizing sector, including a choice among tied minimizers.
- **I_posteriors**: The observed density and the fresh conditional core belong to distinct conditional laws; potential matching occurs after expectation.
- **I_bracketing**: Fixed-sector energy differences are recovered by integration of the priced law and monotone sector bracketing; convexity of sector energies is not a hypothesis.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A_fixed**: Use the theorem to claim convergence of ionization energy at a fixed removed-electron number. -> The joint limit and the outer iterated limits do not establish that fixed-number convergence.
- **A_subtraction**: Estimate the smaller ionization difference by subtracting leading total-energy asymptotics. -> The uncontrolled remainder may exceed the desired energy difference.
- **A_software**: Transfer the ionization power law to software resource allocation, workload scaling, or reliability. -> No identification of those observables with the spectral ionization energy is established.
- **A_posterior**: Replace the fresh conditional core by the observation posterior in the local comparison. -> This merges distinct conditional laws before the averaged Coulomb-potential transfer is justified.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The energy conclusion is an asymptotic law for full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms in the joint regime where the removed-electron number and the nuclear-charge ratio tend to infinity. The limiting field is obtained by subsequential compactness and a slow diagonal choice of comparison parameters. These arguments do not supply a finite constructive evaluation of the spectral infimum or the exterior charge with an error certificate; convergence at a fixed removed-electron number is explicitly not asserted.

| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The kinetic and Coulomb energies belong to an atomic Hamiltonian; no accelerator execution model or hardware cost is specified. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem concerns nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms and does not specify a cryptographic primitive or security adversary. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional observations are used to compare electron densities and fields; no learning objective or statistical learning guarantee is given. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The priced minimum is an atomic variational problem; no reduction from a scheduling or resource-allocation objective is established. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conditional expectation tower belongs to the probability law of an atomic state; no communication or distributed coordination model is provided. |
| other (atomic_physics) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The theorem directly determines the leading ionization energy for full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states when the removed-electron number and the nuclear-charge-to-removed-electron ratio both tend to infinity. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L_model** (hypothesis): The energy theorem is restricted to full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states in atomic units. Blocked use: ['Relativistic atoms, altered spin conventions, molecular nuclei, or nuclear dynamics without a separate theorem.']
- **L_joint** (hypothesis): The main quantum energy conclusion requires the stated joint large removed-electron-number and large nuclear-charge-ratio regime. Blocked use: ['Finite-input error guarantees or extrapolation to a regime outside the stated hypotheses.']
- **L_fixed** (edge_case): Convergence of the ionization energy at fixed removed-electron number is not asserted or assumed. Blocked use: ['Replacing the inner upper and lower limits by an established ordinary limit at fixed removed-electron number.']
- **L_subtract** (approximation): Subtracting leading total-energy asymptotics does not control the smaller ionization difference. Blocked use: ['Certifying the ionization energy solely by subtracting leading total-energy approximations.']
- **L_offset** (hypothesis): The positive-price comparison must retain dependence on the growing energy offset above the unpriced minimum. Blocked use: ['Applying a fixed-offset screening conclusion to priced minimizing states without verifying its hypotheses.']
- **L_posteriors** (hypothesis): The observation posterior and the fresh-cut conditional core are distinct laws; their potentials are matched only after expectation. Blocked use: ['Identifying the two posteriors before the averaged potential transfer.']
- **L_sector** (edge_case): The fixed-sector argument uses monotonicity without requiring convexity or every sector to minimize at some price. Blocked use: ['Assuming sector convexity or assuming a price selects every desired particle number.']
- **L_profile** (hypothesis): Uniqueness of the limiting profile is stated for classical solutions with uniform decay and positive singular bounds near the origin. Blocked use: ['Applying the uniqueness conclusion after discarding the singularity or the boundary condition at infinity.']
- **L_constructive** (nonconstructive): The limiting-field construction uses a slow diagonal choice and selected conditional data; it supplies no certified finite evaluation of the spectral energy or exterior charge. Blocked use: ['Presenting the limiting construction as a terminating solver with certified finite accuracy.']
- **L_transfer** (counterexample_scope): A mathematical reduction from software observables to this atomic spectral problem is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using the atomic ionization power law to predict computing capacity, service performance, or reliability by analogy.']
- **L_formal** (formal_scope): A machine-checked proof certificate for the full theorem is ABSENT from the provided paper. Blocked use: ['Describing literal extraction or finite arithmetic checks as formal verification of the many-body asymptotic theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 263 (`lean/docs/263.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Generalized ionization energies for full Coulomb atoms](../../preprints/Generalized-ionization-energies-for-full-Coulomb-atoms-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Generalized outer-electron radii of neutral Coulomb atoms](../../preprints/Generalized-outer-electron-radii-of-neutral-Coulomb-atoms-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives the large-ionization asymptotics for the full nonrelativistic two-spin Coulomb atom. Let $I_m(Z)$ be the energy needed to remove $m$ electrons from a neutral atom of nuclear charge $Z$. There is a positive coefficient $a_{\mathrm{TF}}$ with the Thomas–Fermi variational characterization such that $I_m(Z)/m^{7/3}\to a_{\mathrm{TF}}$ whenever $m\to\infty$ and $Z/m\to\infty$, with integers $Z>m\ge1$. The corresponding iterated limsup and liminf limits hold with $Z\to\infty$ first. The energy uses the full antisymmetric Sobolev form domain; fixed-$m$ convergence and ground-state attainment are outside this statement.
>
> For neutral Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states, the formalization proves the asymptotic law for generalized outer-electron radii defined by an expected exterior electron mass $m$. For every choice of normalized ground states, both the upper and lower large-nuclear-charge radius limits satisfy
> $m^{1/3}R_m\longrightarrow (81\pi^2/2)^{1/3}$ as $m\to\infty$. The large-nuclear-charge limit is taken first. Ground states are inputs to the statement; their existence is not asserted separately.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory Filter
open scoped Topology BigOperators ContDiff

noncomputable section
namespace CoulombAtom

abbrev Space := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)
abbrev Configuration (N : ℕ) := Fin N → Space
abbrev Spins (N : ℕ) := Fin N → Fin 2

structure FormVector (N : ℕ) where
  value : Spins N → Configuration N → ℂ
  gradient : Spins N → Fin N → Fin 3 → Configuration N → ℂ

def direction {N : ℕ} (i : Fin N) (a : Fin 3) : Configuration N :=
  Pi.single i (EuclideanSpace.single a 1)

def FormAdmissible {N : ℕ} (ψ : FormVector N) : Prop :=
  (∀ s, MemLp (ψ.value s) 2) ∧
  (∀ s i a, MemLp (ψ.gradient s i a) 2) ∧
  (∀ s i a (φ : Configuration N → ℝ),
    ContDiff ℝ ∞ φ → HasCompactSupport φ →
    (∫ x, ψ.value s x * Complex.ofReal (lineDeriv ℝ φ x (direction i a))) =
      -(∫ x, ψ.gradient s i a x * (φ x : ℂ))) ∧
  (∀ (π : Equiv.Perm (Fin N)) s, ∀ᵐ x,
    ψ.value (s ∘ π) (x ∘ π) =
      (((Equiv.Perm.sign π : ℤ) : ℂ) * ψ.value s x)) ∧
  (∑ s : Spins N, ∫ x, ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2) = 1 ∧
  (∀ s i, Integrable (fun x => ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2 / ‖x i‖)) ∧
  (∀ s i j, i ≠ j →
    Integrable (fun x => ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2 / ‖x i - x j‖))

def formEnergy {N : ℕ} (Z : ℝ) (ψ : FormVector N) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) *
      (∑ s : Spins N, ∑ i : Fin N, ∑ a : Fin 3,
        (∫ x, ‖ψ.gradient s i a x‖ ^ 2)) -
    Z * (∑ s : Spins N, ∑ i : Fin N,
      (∫ x, ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2 / ‖x i‖)) +
    (∑ s : Spins N, ∑ i : Fin N, ∑ j : Fin N,
      if i < j then (∫ x, ‖ψ.value s x‖ ^ 2 / ‖x i - x j‖) else 0)

def energy (Z : ℝ) (N : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if N = 0 then 0 else sInf {e | ∃ ψ : FormVector N,
    FormAdmissible ψ ∧ formEnergy Z ψ = e}

def ionization (m Z : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  energy Z (Z - m) - energy Z Z

def tfKinetic : ℝ := (3 / 10 : ℝ) * (3 * Real.pi ^ 2) ^ (2 / 3 : ℝ)

def directIntegrand (ρ : Space → ℝ) (p : Space × Space) : ℝ :=
  ρ p.1 * ρ p.2 / ‖p.1 - p.2‖

def TFAdmissible (M : ℝ) (ρ : Space → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∀ᵐ x, 0 ≤ ρ x) ∧ Integrable ρ ∧ (∫ x, ρ x) = M ∧
  Integrable (fun x => ρ x ^ (5 / 3 : ℝ)) ∧
  Integrable (fun x => ρ x / ‖x‖) ∧ Integrable (directIntegrand ρ)

def tfFunctional (Z : ℝ) (ρ : Space → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  tfKinetic * (∫ x, ρ x ^ (5 / 3 : ℝ)) - Z * (∫ x, ρ x / ‖x‖) +
    (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∫ p, directIntegrand ρ p

def tfEnergy (Z M : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {e | ∃ ρ : Space → ℝ, TFAdmissible M ρ ∧ tfFunctional Z ρ = e}

def tfIonization (m Z : ℝ) : ℝ := tfEnergy Z (Z - m) - tfEnergy Z Z

def TFCharacterization (a : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ m : ℝ, 0 < m →
    Tendsto (tfIonization m) atTop (𝓝 (a * m ^ (7 / 3 : ℝ)))

def JointLimit (a : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ m Z : ℕ → ℕ, (∀ j, 1 ≤ m j ∧ m j < Z j) →
    Tendsto m atTop atTop →
    Tendsto (fun j => (Z j : ℝ) / (m j : ℝ)) atTop atTop →
    Tendsto (fun j => ionization (m j) (Z j) / (m j : ℝ) ^ (7 / 3 : ℝ))
      atTop (𝓝 a)

def IteratedLimits (a : ℝ) : Prop :=
  Tendsto (fun m : ℕ => (limsup (ionization m) atTop) / (m : ℝ) ^ (7 / 3 : ℝ))
    atTop (𝓝 a) ∧
  Tendsto (fun m : ℕ => (liminf (ionization m) atTop) / (m : ℝ) ^ (7 / 3 : ℝ))
    atTop (𝓝 a)

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ a : ℝ, 0 < a ∧ TFCharacterization a ∧ JointLimit a ∧ IteratedLimits a

theorem generalized_ionization : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end CoulombAtom

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the energy part of the generalized ionization conjecture for full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states. The energy needed to remove <i>m</i> electrons is asymptotic to $`a_{\mathrm{TF}}m^{7/3}`$ whenever $`m\to\infty`$ and $`Z/m\to\infty`$, with the Thomas–Fermi constant in atomic units. We also obtain both conjectured iterated limits.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The ionization energy of an atom measures the cost of removing electrons while leaving its nucleus fixed. The first few electrons removed belong to the outer part of the atom, whereas the leading total energy is largely determined by the much denser inner region. Thomas–Fermi theory, introduced by Thomas and Fermi (Thomas 1927; Fermi 1927) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Thomas1927](#ref-Thomas1927), [Fermi1927](#ref-Fermi1927)], describes the leading large-charge energy. Lieb and Simon established this connection to the many-body Schrödinger theory (Lieb and Simon 1973, 1977) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[LiebSimon1973](#ref-LiebSimon1973), [LiebSimon1977](#ref-LiebSimon1977)]. Their energy theorem (Lieb and Simon 1977, Theorem III.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LiebSimon1977](#ref-LiebSimon1977)] concerns terms of order $Z^{7/3}$. Subtracting two such total-energy asymptotics does not control a much smaller ionization energy: the remainder may exceed the difference being sought.

The generalized ionization conjecture asserts that Thomas–Fermi theory nevertheless determines the leading cost of removing many outer electrons, provided their number remains small compared with the nuclear charge. Solovej formulated this statement through upper and lower large-$Z$ limits, followed by a large removed-electron-number limit (Solovej 2016, sec. 3, Equation (1)) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Solovej2016](#ref-Solovej2016)]. We prove its energy part for the full two-spin Coulomb Hamiltonian, in the stronger joint regime in which both the number removed and the ratio of nuclear charge to that number tend to infinity.

Earlier full-model estimates cover different ionization regimes. Seco, Sigal and Solovej bounded the first ionization energy of a neutral atom by $CZ^{20/21}$, using the improvement recorded in their note added in proof (Seco, Sigal, and Solovej 1990, Theorem 1 and p. 315) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[SecoSigalSolovej1990](#ref-SecoSigalSolovej1990)]. Ivrii’s estimates imply agreement with the magnitude of the Thomas–Fermi chemical potential for successive one-electron ionization energies of positive atoms when $Z^{5/7}=o(Z-N)$ and $Z-N=o(Z)$, where $N$ is the number of electrons (Ivrii 2017, Theorem 6.2.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ivrii2017](#ref-Ivrii2017)]. These results leave open the joint neutral-to-positive-ion energy asymptotic addressed here.

Uniform excess-charge bounds were proved in Thomas–Fermi–von Weizsäcker theory by Benguria and Lieb (Benguria and Lieb 1985) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BenguriaLieb1985](#ref-BenguriaLieb1985)]; Solovej proved uniform ionization bounds in reduced Hartree–Fock theory (Solovej 1991) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Solovej1991](#ref-Solovej1991)] <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)] and then in unrestricted Hartree–Fock theory (Solovej 2003). The Thomas–Fermi–von Weizsäcker and Hartree–Fock results concern different variational problems. The passage to arbitrary correlated fermionic states requires additional screening estimates, obtained here through conditional many-body states.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The theorem and its normalization

<a id="eq:hamiltonian"></a>
<a id="eq:tf-constant"></a>
In atomic units, let $Z$ be a positive integer and let $$\label{eq:hamiltonian}
 H_{Z,N}
 =\sum_{i=1}^N\left(-\frac12\Delta_i-\frac{Z}{|x_i|}\right)
   +\sum_{1\le i<l\le N}\frac1{|x_i-x_l|}$$ act on $\bigwedge^N L^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb C^2)$. Write $E_Z(N)=\inf\sigma(H_{Z,N})$ and $E_Z(0)=0$. For integers $Z>m\ge1$, define $$I_m(Z)=E_Z(Z-m)-E_Z(Z).$$ The kinetic coefficient of the corresponding Thomas–Fermi functional is $T=\frac3{10}(3\pi^2)^{2/3}$. More explicitly, put $$\mathcal T_Z(\rho)
 =T\int_{\mathbb R^3}\rho^{5/3}
 -Z\int_{\mathbb R^3}\frac{\rho(x)}{|x|}\,dx
 +\frac12\iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
          \frac{\rho(x)\rho(y)}{|x-y|}\,dx\,dy,$$ and let $E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(N)$ be its infimum over nonnegative densities of mass $N$ and finite displayed terms. Set $I_m^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)=E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z-m)-E_Z^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)$. The constant in the conjecture is characterized by the classical Thomas–Fermi weak-ionization law (Bénilan and Brezis 2004, sec. 6, Corollary 5) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BenilanBrezis2004](#ref-BenilanBrezis2004)]: $$\label{eq:tf-constant}
 \lim_{Z\to\infty}I_m^{\mathrm{TF}}(Z)=a_{\mathrm{TF}}m^{7/3},\qquad m>0.$$ Our identification of the coefficient in <a href="#sec:fixed-sectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed-sectors">6</a> recovers this characterization in the present normalization.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:joint-limit"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Generalized ionization energy). *For full nonrelativistic Coulomb atoms with two electron spin states, $$\label{eq:joint-limit}
 \frac{I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}}\longrightarrow a_{\mathrm{TF}}>0
 \quad\text{whenever}\quad m\longrightarrow\infty,
 \qquad \frac Zm\longrightarrow\infty,$$ where $Z>m\ge1$ are integers. In particular, <a id="eq:iterated-upper"></a><a id="eq:iterated-lower"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \lim_{m\to\infty}
  \frac{\limsup_{Z\to\infty} I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}}
   &=a_{\mathrm{TF}},\label{eq:iterated-upper}\\
 \lim_{m\to\infty}
  \frac{\liminf_{Z\to\infty} I_m(Z)}{m^{7/3}}
   &=a_{\mathrm{TF}}.\label{eq:iterated-lower}
\end{aligned}$$ The inner limits are taken over integer $Z$ with $m$ fixed. No convergence of $I_m(Z)$ at a fixed $m$ is asserted or assumed.*

</div>

The limits in [eq:iterated-upper,eq:iterated-lower] [eq:iterated-upper](#eq:iterated-upper), [eq:iterated-lower](#eq:iterated-lower) are precisely the energy part of the generalized ionization conjecture formulated by Solovej (Solovej 2016, sec. 3, Equation (1)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Solovej2016](#ref-Solovej2016)]. The joint assertion <a href="#eq:joint-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:joint-limit">[eq:joint-limit]</a> strengthens that order of limits. For historical comparison, Solovej established uniform first-ionization upper bounds and sharp generalized radius estimates in unrestricted Hartree–Fock theory (Solovej 2003, Theorems 3.8 and 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)]; the generalized conjecture and its Hartree–Fock history are discussed in (Solovej 2016, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Solovej2016](#ref-Solovej2016)]. Here the energy is the exact spectral infimum of <a href="#eq:hamiltonian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:hamiltonian">[eq:hamiltonian]</a>, with no restriction to Slater determinants.

For this same full two-spin Coulomb model, the companion (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] also proves $0<c\le E_Z(Z-1)-E_Z(Z)\le C<\infty$ uniformly for every integer $Z\ge1$. This first-ionization bound does not assert convergence at fixed $m$ and is distinct from the large-$m$ limits above.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A price per electron

The proof first allows the particle number to vary. For fixed $Z$, abbreviate $E_n=E_Z(n)$, and introduce the unconstrained energy and the priced minimum $$E=\inf_{n\ge0}E_n,\qquad
 P(\lambda)=\min_{n\ge0}\bigl(E_n+\lambda n\bigr),\qquad\lambda>0.$$ The minimum exists, as verified in <a href="#sec:pricing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pricing">2</a>. Its advantage is local: replacing the exterior electrons by a trial state need not preserve their number. The price accounts for the change exactly. At the length scale $s=\lambda^{-1/4}$, the removed charge is of order $s^{-3}$. The main intermediate result identifies its coefficient for every minimizing sector, including at prices where several sectors minimize.

<a id="stmt-thm-priced-deficit"></a>
<div id="thm:priced-deficit" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Deficit at a prescribed price). *There is a universal number $q>0$ such that, for every sequence satisfying $$\lambda=s^{-4},\qquad s\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad Zs^3\longrightarrow\infty,$$ and every choice of $n$ minimizing $E_n+\lambda n$, one has $$s^3(Z-n)\longrightarrow q.$$ The number $q$ is the exterior Coulomb charge of the unique classical solution, tending uniformly to zero at infinity, of $$\Delta F=4\pi k\bigl((F-1)_+\bigr)^{3/2}
 \quad\text{on }\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\},
 \qquad k=(5T/3)^{-3/2},$$ that is bounded above and below by positive multiples of $|x|^{-4}$ near zero. The density $k((F-1)_+)^{3/2}$ has bounded support, and $F(x)=q/|x|$ outside that support. Moreover, $$a_{\mathrm{TF}}=\frac37q^{-4/3}.$$*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Analytic precedents and proof structure

The local kinetic comparison combines two classical constructions. Coherent wave packets provide the upper semiclassical energy (Lieb 1981, sec. V.A.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Lieb1981ThomasFermi](#ref-Lieb1981ThomasFermi)]; see also (Solovej 2003, Lemma 8.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)]. Neumann cube eigenvalues and their lattice count supply a lower comparison with the same leading coefficient (Lieb and Simon 1977, Theorems III.10–III.13) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[LiebSimon1977](#ref-LiebSimon1977)]. Their remainders are controlled by local particle counts. This is different from the coarse Lieb–Thirring kinetic inequality (Lieb and Thirring 1975) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[LiebThirring1975](#ref-LiebThirring1975)], whose positive universal constant is sufficient for preliminary density bounds; the spectral splitting proof used in the companion follows Rumin (Rumin 2011, Theorem 1.4 and Section 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Rumin2011](#ref-Rumin2011)].

Screened exterior potentials and outward Thomas–Fermi comparisons are central to Solovej’s Hartree–Fock argument (Solovej 2003, secs. 6, 10, and 12) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)]. Our use of conditional states requires a separate transfer from local quantum energies to conditional fields, while accounting for changes in particle number. The eventual singular profile belongs to the classical Sommerfeld comparison theory (Lieb and Simon 1977, sec. IV) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[LiebSimon1977](#ref-LiebSimon1977)], including the nonspherical comparisons and chemical-potential estimates of (Solovej 2003, Lemma 4.4, Remark 4.5, Theorem 4.6, and Corollary 4.7) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)]. The issue here is to obtain that profile from the correlated quantum state and preserve its positive singularity through conditioning.

We use the localization, screening, and Thomas–Fermi estimates proved in the companion *Uniform excess charge for Coulomb molecules and the outer radius of neutral atoms* (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. In particular, its uniform neutral-sector bound $E_Z(Z)-E\le C$ fixes the zero-price integration constant, and its local-field estimates retain explicit control of an arbitrary energy offset (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 12.3 and Proposition 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. The required positive-price conditional comparisons and barrier propagation are proved in [sec:comparison,sec:barriers] [sec:comparison](#sec:comparison), [sec:barriers](#sec:barriers). Their offset dependence is essential: a minimizing priced state has energy at most $E+Cs^{-7}$, so the allowed offset grows as $s\to0$. A fixed-offset conclusion from the neutral-atom theory cannot by itself yield <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

obtains the rough deficit bound and proves that the rescaled exterior electron mass vanishes beyond a fixed radius. In <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">3</a>, noisy observations of the electrons permit local conditional comparisons while controlling the cost of conditioning. A Thomas–Fermi trial state in a small ball is compared with the priced many-body state. A fresh spatial cut records the local particles and leaves a core outside the patch, whose conditional screened potential is harmonic on the patch. Matching this core comparison to the noisy-observation law yields simultaneous upper and lower implications between local density and local potential. Two posterior laws occur in this argument; keeping them distinct is necessary for the Coulomb comparison.

The remaining difficulty is to retain the singular inner charge through successive averaging. [sec:barriers](#sec:barriers) constructs a positive subsolution at the innermost scale and propagates it through the observation scales. The construction pays for exceptional conditional data with an error density of vanishing expected mass. The weak differential inequality is proved on open neighborhoods of the retained branches before taking their maximum, so no derivative is restricted to an activity interface. [sec:limit](#sec:limit) then selects conditional data, rescales by $s$, and obtains a decaying solution of the unit-price Thomas–Fermi equation. The barrier prevents loss of the singularity. A maximum-principle argument identifies its Sommerfeld coefficient and proves uniqueness, which forces the same deficit for every minimizing sector.

Finally, <a href="#sec:fixed-sectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed-sectors">6</a> integrates the slopes of the priced minimum and brackets the desired particle number between two minimizing sectors. Only monotonicity of $E_Z(N)$ is used in this step; convexity in $N$ is not needed. The analogous Thomas–Fermi calculation identifies the coefficient with <a href="#eq:tf-constant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:tf-constant">[eq:tf-constant]</a>. A sequential argument then gives the specified order of limits in [eq:iterated-upper,eq:iterated-lower] [eq:iterated-upper](#eq:iterated-upper), [eq:iterated-lower](#eq:iterated-lower).

<a id="sec:pricing"></a>
### Priced sectors and an exterior cutoff

<a id="eq:scaling-regime"></a>
The price per electron permits insertion and deletion comparisons without a constraint on the exterior particle number. We first collect the screening estimates that remain applicable when the energy above the unpriced minimum grows. Throughout, write $$K(x)=\frac1{|x|},\qquad V=ZK,\qquad \nu=Z\delta_0,\qquad
 D(f)=\frac12\iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
                  \frac{f(x)f(y)}{|x-y|}\,dx\,dy,$$ and use the constants $$T=\frac3{10}(3\pi^2)^{2/3},\qquad
 k=\left(\frac{5T}{3}\right)^{-3/2}.$$ These are the Thomas–Fermi constants for two spin states and kinetic energy $-\Delta/2$. For a fixed integer $Z$, abbreviate $E_n=E_Z(n)$, put $E_0=0$, and define $$E=\inf_{n\ge0}E_n,\qquad
 P(\lambda)=\min_{n\ge0}(E_n+\lambda n)\quad(\lambda>0).$$ The existence of this minimum follows below. The asymptotic regime used until <a href="#sec:fixed-sectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed-sectors">6</a> is $$\label{eq:scaling-regime}
 \lambda=s^{-4},\qquad s\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad Zs^3\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Every assertion concerning a minimizing sector applies to every choice of minimizing index at every system in this regime.

If $\psi$ is a normalized finite-sector form-domain vector, we call it an *unshifted $\delta$-state* when its form energy is at most $E+\delta$, where $\delta\ge0$. Its position law is $|\psi|^2$, with the spin variables summed unless they are recorded; its one-particle density is denoted by $\rho_\psi$. Expectations may also include the auxiliary random labels specified below. For a real number $v$, write $v_+=\max(v,0)$ and $v_-=\max(-v,0)$. Constants $C,c>0$ can change from line to line. Unless a dependence is displayed, they depend only on this fixed model and on the fixed cutoff and kernel conventions.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Imported estimates and their hypotheses

<a id="stmt-lem-sector-inputs"></a>
<div id="lem:sector-inputs" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:neutral-offset"></a>
<a id="eq:ground-multiplier"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Sector and energy estimates). *The sequence $E_n$ is nonincreasing. If $E_n<E_{n-1}$, there is a normalized ground state in sector $n$, and $n\le2Z+1\le3Z$. There is a largest such index $N_*$, with $Z\le N_*\le3Z$, and $$E=E_{N_*}=E_n\quad(n\ge N_*).$$ There are universal $C,Z_0$ such that $$\label{eq:neutral-offset}
 E_Z(Z)\le E+C\qquad(Z\ge Z_0).$$ If $\Psi$ is a normalized sector ground state and $F$ is a bounded real symmetric Lipschitz function of the spatial configuration, then $$\label{eq:ground-multiplier}
 \langle F\Psi,H_{Z,n}F\Psi\rangle
       -E_n\|F\Psi\|_2^2
       =\frac12\mathbb E_\Psi|\nabla F|^2,$$ where the left side is interpreted as a quadratic form and the gradient includes all spatial coordinates. Finally, every unshifted $\delta$-state in a sector $n\le3Z$ satisfies <a id="eq:global-density-input"></a>$$\label{eq:global-density-input}
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}\rho_\psi^{5/3}\le C(Z^{7/3}+\delta).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sector-inputs"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sector-inputs</summary>

*Proof.* The sector assertions and the multiplier identity are (OpenAI 2026, Lemmas 2.1 and 2.4–2.6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]; the density estimate is (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. The stronger estimate $E_Z(Z-1)\le E+C$ is (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 12.3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. Monotonicity gives <a href="#eq:neutral-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:neutral-offset">[eq:neutral-offset]</a>. All these results use exactly the Hamiltonian and the two-spin convention fixed here. ◻

</details>

A one-particle square cut consists of nonnegative bounded-gradient Lipschitz functions $o,c$ with $o^2+c^2=1$. Conditional on the positions, assign the particles independently to the out and core groups with probabilities $o^2$ and $c^2$. The *cut data* record the out labels, positions, and spins. For a finite sequence of cuts, stage labels are recorded as well. Conditional core states are obtained by the corresponding products of cut factors, so antisymmetry is preserved within each group. This is the localization convention of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)].

The local estimate needed for the later patch comparison has a quantitative condition on these cuts. Fix, once and for all, $$L=10^5,\qquad A=40,\qquad a_y=|y|/L,\qquad B_y=B(y,a_y)
       \quad(y\ne0).$$ For an unshifted $\delta$-state put $$\begin{gathered}
 m_y=\max(a_y^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta a_y},\qquad
 J_y=V(y)-\sum_{|x_i-y|\ge Aa_y}\frac1{|y-x_i|},\\
 P_* =\max\left(1,\sup_{y\ne0}
                 \frac{\mathbb E\bigl[\#\{i:x_i\in B_y\}^2\bigr]}{m_y^2}\right).
 \end{gathered}$$ The number $P_*$ is finite for each finite system, since the count is at most the sector particle number and $m_y\ge1$.

<a id="stmt-lem-local-field-input"></a>
<div id="lem:local-field-input" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Local fields for arbitrary offsets). *For every normalized unshifted $\delta$-state of finite particle number, $P_*\le C$. Fix $y\ne0$ and $C_0\ge1$. The data $X$ may be trivial. Otherwise, suppose that the initial cut, or finite sequence of cuts $(o_h,c_h)$, has total out support covered by at most $C_0$ cells $B_z$ with $$C_0^{-1}\le a_z/a_y\le C_0,
 \qquad
 \sum_h\bigl(|\nabla o_h|^2+|\nabla c_h|^2\bigr)
       \le\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\mathbf 1_{B_z},
 \qquad
 \frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\le C_0\sqrt{P_*}.$$ Then, for the complete cut data, <a id="eq:local-field-input"></a>$$\label{eq:local-field-input}
 \left\|(\mathbb E[J_y\mid X])_+\right\|_2
       \le C(C_0)\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ The constants are independent of $Z$, the sector, the state, $y$, and $\delta$, apart from the displayed dependence through $m_y$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-local-field-input"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-local-field-input</summary>

*Proof.* This is (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. Its offset is measured from the minimum $E$ over all sectors, just as here; it has no restriction that the offset stay bounded along a sequence. In all applications below we verify the stronger cut condition $D_z^2/(a_zm_z)\le C_0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-screening-inputs"></a>
<div id="lem:screening-inputs" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:annular-count"></a>
<a id="eq:conditional-inner-field"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Annular screening estimates). *Let $\psi$ be a normalized unshifted $\delta$-state of finite particle number. For fixed $0<\alpha<\beta<\infty$ and every $u>0$, $$\label{eq:annular-count}
 \left\|\#\{i:\alpha u\le|x_i|\le\beta u\}\right\|_2
   \le C_{\alpha,\beta}
           \bigl(\max(u^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta u}\bigr).$$ Suppose in addition that $n\le3Z$, and set $$B_{u/8}(x)=V(x)-\sum_{|x_i|<u/8}\frac1{|x-x_i|}.$$ Let $Y$ be trivial or be the data of a square cut whose out support lies in $\{u/4\le|x|\le8u\}$ and whose squared gradient sum is at most $C/u^2$. There are conditional versions harmonic on $u/4<|x|<6u$ for which $$\label{eq:conditional-inner-field}
 \left\|\sup_{u/2\le|x|\le4u}
              \bigl(\mathbb E[B_{u/8}(x)\mid Y]\bigr)_+\right\|_2
 \le C\left(\frac{\max(u^{-3},1)}u+\sqrt{\frac\delta u}\right).$$ The constant in the second estimate may depend on the fixed gradient bound. Both estimates allow every finite $\delta$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-screening-inputs"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-screening-inputs</summary>

*Proof.* The count estimate is (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 4.9) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. Boundary spheres have zero probability because the one-particle density is absolutely continuous, so open or closed annuli give the same estimate. For the field bound, use (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 12.1) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] with inner cutoff $h=u/8$, broad annulus $u/4<|x|<6u$, and smaller annulus $u/2\le|x|\le4u$. The series in that lemma is $$\frac{\max(u^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta u}}u
 +\sum_{j\ge0}
       \frac{\max((2^jh)^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta\,2^jh}}
            {\max(u,2^jh)}.$$ With $h=u/8$, geometric summation bounds it by the right side of <a href="#eq:conditional-inner-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:conditional-inner-field">[eq:conditional-inner-field]</a>, also when $u<1$. The permitted cut support and gradient bound satisfy the fixed-annulus hypotheses of that lemma. The conditional harmonic versions follow by integration against the conditional law: for each fixed finite system the inner sources are uniformly separated from the annulus, so differentiation under the integral is valid. No minimizing property is required in either estimate. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The rough charge deficit

<a id="stmt-lem-priced-rough"></a>
<div id="lem:priced-rough" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:rough-deficit"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *The minimum $P(\lambda)$ is attained. Along <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, every minimizing index $n$ is eventually nonzero and strictly bound. For a normalized ground state $\Psi$ in that sector, put $D_0=E_n-E$. Uniformly over all these choices, $$\label{eq:rough-deficit}
 |Z-n|\le Cs^{-3},\qquad 0\le D_0\le Cs^{-7}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-priced-rough"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-priced-rough</summary>

*Proof.* By <a href="#lem:sector-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sector-inputs">3</a>, $E_n$ is constant for $n\ge N_*$. Since $\lambda>0$, the priced objective is strictly increasing thereafter, so its minimum is attained. If a minimizing index is positive, comparison with $n-1$ gives $$E_{n-1}-E_n\ge\lambda>0.$$ It is therefore strictly bound, has a ground state, and satisfies $n\le3Z$. A normalized exponential one-electron trial of radius $Z^{-1}$ has energy $-Z^2/2$. Moreover, $$\frac\lambda{Z^2}=\frac{s^2}{(Zs^3)^2}\longrightarrow0.$$ Thus $P(\lambda)<0$ eventually, excluding the vacuum.

<a id="eq:priced-offset"></a>
The neutral sector is an admissible competitor. By <a href="#eq:neutral-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:neutral-offset">[eq:neutral-offset]</a>, $$\label{eq:priced-offset}
 0\le D_0\le C+s^{-4}(Z-n).$$ For a fixed inner configuration, the spherical average of $B_{s/8}$ on $|x|=s$ equals $(Z-\#\{|x_i|<s/8\})/s$. Its expectation is at least $(Z-n)/s$. Apply <a href="#eq:conditional-inner-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:conditional-inner-field">[eq:conditional-inner-field]</a> with trivial data and offset $D_0$ to obtain, for small $s$, $$Z-n\le C\bigl(s^{-3}+\sqrt{D_0s}\bigr).$$ If $d=Z-n\ge0$ and $z=s^3d$, then <a href="#eq:priced-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:priced-offset">[eq:priced-offset]</a> gives $D_0s^7\le Cs^7+z$, whence $$z\le C\bigl(1+\sqrt{z+Cs^7}\bigr).$$ This bounds $z$. If $d<0$, <a href="#eq:priced-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:priced-offset">[eq:priced-offset]</a> instead gives $d\ge-Cs^4$. These two cases prove the deficit estimate, and substitution in <a href="#eq:priced-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:priced-offset">[eq:priced-offset]</a> proves the bound for $D_0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### A cutoff at one fixed exterior scale

<a id="stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff"></a>
<div id="lem:exterior-cutoff" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:exterior-cutoff"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *There is a sufficiently large fixed $R_0$ such that, for every sequence of minimizing sectors and corresponding ground states in <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, $$\label{eq:exterior-cutoff}
 s^3\mathbb E_\Psi\#\{i:|x_i|>R_0s\}\longrightarrow0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-exterior-cutoff</summary>

<a id="eq:shell-eigen-equation"></a>
*Proof.* Choose a smooth $0\le\chi\le1$ supported in $[1/2,4]$ and equal to one on $[1,2]$. At shell scale $u>0$, write $$w_i=\chi(|x_i|/u)^2,\quad S_u=\sum_iw_i,\quad m_u=\mathbb ES_u,
 \quad N_u=\mathbb E\#\{i:u/2\le|x_i|\le4u\}.$$ Test the weak eigen-equation with $S_u\Psi$. In its $i$th summand the other coordinates have energy at least $E_{n-1}$. Integration by parts gives for the selected-coordinate kinetic term $$\frac12\operatorname{Re}\int\nabla_i\overline\Psi
                         \cdot\nabla_i(w_i\Psi)
 =\frac12\int w_i|\nabla_i\Psi|^2
       -\frac14\mathbb E\Delta_iw_i
 \ge-\frac14\mathbb E\Delta_iw_i.$$ Retain only the repulsions from particles in $|x_l|<u/8$; the selected particle lies outside that ball whenever $w_i>0$. Since $E_{n-1}-E_n\ge s^{-4}$, we obtain $$\label{eq:shell-eigen-equation}
 s^{-4}m_u\le Cu^{-2}N_u
                 +\mathbb E\sum_iw_iB_{u/8}(x_i).$$ All these tests are bounded symmetric form-domain multipliers for each fixed system.

<a id="eq:shell-bias-offset"></a>
Suppose $m_u>0$. Bias the position law by $S_u/m_u$, equivalently use the normalized state $$\Psi_u=\sqrt{S_u/m_u}\,\Psi.$$ The function $\sqrt{S_u}$ is the Euclidean norm of the vector of cutoffs. It is Lipschitz, including at its zeros, and $$|\nabla\sqrt{S_u}|^2
       \le Cu^{-2}\#\{i:u/2\le|x_i|\le4u\}.$$ The multiplier identity <a href="#eq:ground-multiplier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:ground-multiplier">[eq:ground-multiplier]</a> therefore makes $\Psi_u$ an unshifted state of offset at most $$\label{eq:shell-bias-offset}
 \delta_u=D_0+Cu^{-2}N_u/m_u.$$

<a id="eq:shell-field-bound"></a>
In both laws take the same auxiliary square cut, full out on $u/2\le|x|\le4u$, supported in $u/4\le|x|\le8u$, with gradient bounded by $C/u$. Its data $Y$ determine $S_u$ and every position with positive weight. Biasing by the $Y$-measurable variable $S_u/m_u$ does not change the conditional raw law on $S_u>0$. Consequently the two conditional harmonic fields there are the same. Let $$M(Y)=\sup_{u/2\le|x|\le4u}
            \bigl(\mathbb E[B_{u/8}(x)\mid Y]\bigr)_+$$ be this common conditional supremum. Conditioning first on $Y$ gives $$\mathbb E\sum_iw_iB_{u/8}(x_i)
       \le\mathbb E[S_uM(Y)]
       =m_u\mathbb E_{\Psi_u}M(Y).$$ The equality of conditional fields is used only on positive-bias data; zero-weight summands are omitted. Their harmonic continuity justifies evaluation at the recorded positions. Applying <a href="#eq:conditional-inner-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:conditional-inner-field">[eq:conditional-inner-field]</a> to $\Psi_u$, with <a href="#eq:shell-bias-offset" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:shell-bias-offset">[eq:shell-bias-offset]</a>, now yields $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E\sum_iw_iB_{u/8}(x_i)
 &\le Cm_u\left(\frac{\max(u^{-3},1)}u
                            +\sqrt{D_0/u}\right)
       +Cu^{-3/2}\sqrt{m_uN_u}.
       \label{eq:shell-field-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ If $m_u=0$, the resulting shell estimate is immediate as well.

By <a href="#eq:rough-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:rough-deficit">[eq:rough-deficit]</a>, for $u\ge R_0s$, $$s^4\frac{\max(u^{-3},1)}u\le R_0^{-4}+s^4,
 \qquad
 s^4\sqrt{D_0/u}\le C\sqrt{s/u}\le CR_0^{-1/2}.$$ Choose $R_0$ large and then $s$ small. The terms multiplying $m_u$ in <a href="#eq:shell-field-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:shell-field-bound">[eq:shell-field-bound]</a> can be absorbed in <a href="#eq:shell-eigen-equation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:shell-eigen-equation">[eq:shell-eigen-equation]</a>. As $m_u\le N_u$, the mixed term is at most $Cu^{-3/2}N_u$, and hence $$m_u\le Cs^4(u^{-2}+u^{-3/2})N_u.$$ The annular count bound and <a href="#eq:rough-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:rough-deficit">[eq:rough-deficit]</a> also give $$N_u\le C\bigl(u^{-3}+1+s^{-7/2}\sqrt u\bigr).$$ The shells on which the weights equal one, with $u=2^jR_0s$, $j\ge0$, cover the required exterior. Tonelli’s theorem and the two preceding inequalities give $$\begin{aligned}
 s^3\mathbb E\#\{|x_i|>R_0s\}
 &\le C s^7\sum_{j\ge0}
       (u^{-2}+u^{-3/2})(u^{-3}+1+s^{-7/2}u^{1/2})
       \bigg|_{u=2^jR_0s}\\
 &\le C_{R_0}
       \bigl(s^2+s^{5/2}+s^5+s^{11/2}\bigr)
       \longrightarrow0.
\end{aligned}$$ Every power of $u$ in this sum is negative. Thus the estimate includes arbitrarily distant shells, with the same fixed $R_0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:comparison"></a>
### Conditional density and field comparison at positive price

We now obtain a local Thomas–Fermi relation for a conditional density. The conditioning retains information about the electron configuration while permitting a controlled change of state on any event of appreciable probability. Two different conditional laws enter the argument: observations define the density to be estimated, whereas a fresh spatial cut defines the core used in the energy comparison. We keep these laws separate and match their potentials only after taking expectation.

Throughout this section, $\Psi$ is a normalized ground state in any sector $n$ minimizing $P(\lambda)$, with $\lambda=s^{-4}$ and <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a> in force. In particular, $n\leq 3Z$ and $D_0=E_n-E\leq Cs^{-7}$ by <a href="#lem:priced-rough" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:priced-rough">6</a>. All statements about sufficiently small scales are uniform in the choice of this minimizing sector.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Nested observations and their energy cost

Fix $0<\varepsilon<1$, to be chosen in <a href="#sec:barriers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:barriers">4</a>, and define <a id="eq:observation-scales"></a>$$r_0=\varepsilon Z^{-1/3},\qquad r_j=2^j r_0,\qquad
 J_* =\max\{j\geq0:r_j\leq s\},\qquad
 \ell_j=r_j^{1.01}\quad(0\leq j<J_*).
 \label{eq:observation-scales}$$ Eventually $2r_0\leq s$. At each scale $j<J_*$, observe the unordered array of points $x_i+\ell_jU_{ji}$. All coordinates of all $U_{ji}$ are independent, also independently of the raw configuration, with density $$\zeta(u)=c_\zeta\exp\!\left(-\frac1{1-u^2}\right)
                  \mathbf 1_{\{|u|<1\}}.$$ Labels are forgotten separately in each array. Let $\mathcal F_j$ be the information in arrays $j,\ldots,J_*-1$, and let $\mathcal F_{J_*}$ be trivial. These sigma-fields decrease with $j$.

Use the constants $L=10^5$, $A=40$ and $a_y=|y|/L$ from <a href="#lem:local-field-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-field-input">4</a>. Fix a real radial function $g\in C_c^\infty(B(0,1))$ with $\int g^2=1$, set $g_b(x)=b^{-3/2}g(x/b)$, and choose $$w=10^{-5},\qquad 0<c_1<(10L)^{-1}.$$ Writing $d_x=|x|$, define the master packets and conditional fields by <a id="eq:master-kernel"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 D_x^{\mathrm b}&=\max(d_x,r_0),&
 t_x&=c_1D_x^{\mathrm b}\min(D_x^{\mathrm b},s)^w,\\
 \mathcal K_x(y)&=g_{t_x}(y-x)^2,&
 \mu_j(y)&=\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_i\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)
                                  \,\middle|\,\mathcal F_j\right],\\
 H'_j(y)&=V(y)-\lambda-(K*\mu_j)(y).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:master-kernel}$$ The superscript $\mathrm b$ indicates the lower clamp, not a power. The width function is globally Lipschitz, with $\operatorname{Lip}(t)\leq c_1(1+w)s^w$, and $t_x\geq c_1r_0^{1+w}$. Thus, for each fixed system, $\mu_j$ is smooth in its evaluation variable, has mass $n$, and has deterministic bounds on all spatial derivatives. Its Coulomb potential and first derivatives are also continuous and deterministically bounded. These bounds at a fixed system will justify conditional integration; they are not claimed to be uniform in $Z$.

We use versions obtained by integrating against the conditional law of the raw configuration. Such versions can, for example, be specified by the finite-array likelihoods in (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. They give, simultaneously in space, <a id="eq:observation-tower"></a>$$\mathbb E[\mu_j\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\mu_{j+1},\qquad
 \mathbb E[H'_j\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H'_{j+1},\qquad
 \Delta H'_j=-4\pi\nu+4\pi\mu_j.
 \label{eq:observation-tower}$$ Continuity extends identities first obtained on a countable dense set. In particular, $H'_j$ is subharmonic off the nucleus.

<a id="stmt-lem-event-tilt"></a>
<div id="lem:event-tilt" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:event-tilt-cost"></a>
**Lemma 8** (Cost of an observation event). *Let $j<J_*$ and let $A'\in\mathcal F_j$ have probability $p>0$. There is a normalized antisymmetric state $\Psi_{A'}$ in the same sector whose raw law is the raw marginal conditioned on $A'$ and whose form energy satisfies $$\langle\Psi_{A'},H_{Z,n}\Psi_{A'}\rangle
 \leq E_n+C r_j^{-2.02}\log^5(e/p).
 \label{eq:event-tilt-cost}$$ The constant is independent of the particle number, the number of observed arrays, and $D_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-event-tilt"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-event-tilt</summary>

*Proof.* If $p_{A'}(x)$ is the probability of the observed event conditional on the raw positions, take $\Psi_{A'}=\sqrt{p_{A'}(x)/p}\,\Psi$. The multiplier is symmetric and preserves the form domain. The first inequality of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 5.2) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)], with $\ell_j=r_j^{1.01}$, is exactly <a href="#eq:event-tilt-cost" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:event-tilt-cost">[eq:event-tilt-cost]</a>. Its proof uses the ground-state multiplier identity in the sector $n$; it measures the cost above $E_n$, independently of the offset of $E_n$ from $E$. ◻

</details>

Accordingly, the unshifted offset of the tilted state is at most $D_0+C r_j^{-2.02}\log^5(e/p)$, while its shifted energy is at most <a id="eq:shifted-tilt-cost"></a>$$P(\lambda)+C r_j^{-2.02}\log^5(e/p).
 \label{eq:shifted-tilt-cost}$$ This distinction is what permits the local comparison below for $D_0$ up to order $s^{-7}$.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Statement and preliminary bounds

<a id="stmt-prop-inverse-comparison"></a>
<div id="prop:inverse-comparison" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:inverse-comparison"></a>
**Proposition 9** (Simultaneous inverse comparison). *There is a universal constant $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$ with the following property. Fix $$M\geq2,\qquad 0<h_l<h_h<\infty,\qquad 0<\xi<h_l/16.$$ For all sufficiently far systems along <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, at every $j<J_*$ there is an event $\mathcal G_j\in\mathcal F_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\mathbb P(\mathcal G_j^c)\leq Cr^{25}.$$ On $\mathcal G_j$, the following assertions hold simultaneously for $r\leq d_y\leq4Mr$ and $h_l\leq h\leq h_h$: $$\begin{split}
 d_y^4H'_j(y)&\leq C_{\mathrm{cap}},\\
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2}
       &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\geq h-\xi,\\
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2}
       &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H'_j(y)\leq h+\xi.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:inverse-comparison}$$ The probability constant and the threshold for sufficiently far systems may depend on the displayed fixed parameters and on the fixed packet conventions; $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$ is independent of the band and accuracy parameters.*

</div>

The two implications compare the field with each positive density height from opposite sides. They will yield an approximate Thomas–Fermi relation in <a href="#sec:limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limit">5</a>, while permitting the field itself to be arbitrarily negative where the density is small.

We prove the proposition in the rest of this section. The proof uses the arbitrary-offset screening estimates from <a href="#sec:pricing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pricing">2</a> and the energy cost in <a href="#lem:event-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:event-tilt">8</a>; it does not apply the fixed-offset inverse comparison of (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 10.1) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] with a growing $D_0$.

<a id="eq:packet-scale-ratios"></a>
<a id="eq:packet-approximate-mass"></a>
Fix $j<J_*$ and put $r=r_j$. On the enlarged band $r/2\leq d_y\leq8Mr$, write $$a=a_y,\qquad t=t_y,\qquad \tau=t/a.$$ Uniformly there, for fixed $M$, $$a\asymp_M r,\qquad c_Ma^w\leq\tau\leq Cs^w.
 \label{eq:packet-scale-ratios}$$ If $\mathcal K_x(y)\ne0$, the Lipschitz bound for the widths gives $|x-y|\leq2t$ and $t_x/t=1+O(s^w)$. Changing variables at scale $t$ therefore gives $$\int\mathcal K_z(y)\,dz=1+O(s^w).
 \label{eq:packet-approximate-mass}$$ On the local support, the kernel is bounded by $Ct^{-3}$ and is Lipschitz, in either its center or its evaluation variable, with constant $Ct^{-4}$.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
###### A count event.

<a id="eq:conditional-density-bounds"></a>
Except on an event of probability at most $r^{40}$, the $j$th observed array has at most $C_Mr^{-3}$ entries in the radial band $[r/4,12Mr]$. To prove this, condition on the event that a sufficiently large such threshold is exceeded, if its probability is greater than $r^{40}$. Compact noise support forces every compatible raw configuration to have at least that many points in $[r/8,13Mr]$. By [lem:event-tilt,eq:annular-count] [lem:event-tilt](#lem:event-tilt), [eq:annular-count](#eq:annular-count), its conditional expected count is at most $C_Mr^{-3}$: indeed, $$\sqrt{D_0r}\leq Cr^{-3}(r/s)^{7/2}\leq Cr^{-3},\qquad
 r^{-0.51}\log^{5/2}(e/r^{40})=o(r^{-3}).$$ This is a contradiction for a sufficiently large fixed threshold. Denote the retained event by $\mathcal C_j$. On it every compatible raw configuration has at most $C_Mr^{-3}$ centers relevant to the enlarged band. Integration against the observation posterior consequently gives $$0\leq\mu_j(y)\leq C_Mr^{-6-3w},\qquad
 |\nabla\mu_j(y)|\leq C_Mr^{-7-4w}.
 \label{eq:conditional-density-bounds}$$

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
###### A universal field cap.

<a id="eq:field-cap-tail"></a>
<a id="eq:cap-excess"></a>
<a id="eq:enlarged-field-cap"></a>
At a fixed point $y$ of the enlarged band, condition on $\{d_y^4H'_j(y)>b_0\}$ if it has probability $p>0$. Packets centered outside the exclusion ball in $J_y$ do not contain $y$, by the width bound and the fixed choice of $c_1$. Their potentials at $y$ equal the corresponding point potentials by radial averaging. Dropping the other nonnegative packet potentials and using the observation tower therefore bounds the event-mean of $H'_j(y)$ above by the tilted mean of $J_y-\lambda$. The trivial-data case of <a href="#lem:local-field-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-field-input">4</a> gives $$b_0\leq C+C(d_y/s)^{7/2}-(d_y/s)^4
              +C_Mr^{2.49}\log^{5/2}(e/p).
 \label{eq:field-cap-tail}$$ Here $a<1$ eventually. The polynomial-power difference on the right has a universal upper bound. Choose a universal $C'$ exceeding the entire deterministic bound by one. Solving <a href="#eq:field-cap-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:field-cap-tail">[eq:field-cap-tail]</a> for $p$ and integrating its stretched-exponential tail yields, for every fixed $P>0$, $$\mathbb E\bigl(d_y^4H'_j(y)-C'\bigr)_+
                    \leq C_{P,M}r^P.
 \label{eq:cap-excess}$$ Subharmonicity turns this into a simultaneous bound. For $3r/4\leq d_y\leq6Mr$, take a ball of radius $d_y/10$; it lies in the enlarged band. Its mean bounds $H'_j(y)$ above. The contribution of $C'd_z^{-4}$, after multiplication by $d_y^4$, is bounded by a universal constant, and the remaining contribution is at most $$Cr^{-3}\int_{\{r/2\leq d_z\leq8Mr\}}
                         (d_z^4H'_j(z)-C')_+\,dz.$$ By <a href="#eq:cap-excess" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:cap-excess">[eq:cap-excess]</a> and Markov’s inequality, the latter is at most one outside an event of probability $C_Mr^{40}$. We have proved the simultaneous cap $$H'_j(y)\leq C_{\mathrm{cap}}d_y^{-4}
       \quad(3r/4\leq d_y\leq6Mr)
 \label{eq:enlarged-field-cap}$$ with a universal $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### A fine patch comparison with the price included

<a id="eq:fixed-point-failures"></a>
<a id="eq:patch-offsets"></a>
Fix a point $y$ in the original band and a height $h_l/2\leq h\leq2h_h$. Suppose that one of the following events, intersected with $\mathcal C_j$, has probability $p\geq r^{42}$: $$\begin{array}{ll}
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2},&d_y^4H'_j(y)<h-\xi/4,\\[2pt]
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2},&d_y^4H'_j(y)>h+\xi/4.
 \end{array}
 \label{eq:fixed-point-failures}$$ Write $Q$ for the original joint law conditioned on the event. When spatial cuts are introduced, enlarge this law by fresh labels with their ordinary conditional probabilities given the raw positions. Its raw marginal is the state from <a href="#lem:event-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:event-tilt">8</a>. Set $$\delta'=Cr^{-2.02}\log^5(e/r^{42}),\qquad
 \delta=D_0+\delta'.$$ Then the raw state has unshifted offset at most $\delta$, shifted offset at most $\delta'$, and $$m_y\leq C_Ma^{-3},\qquad
 \delta'=o(a^{-6.98}).
 \label{eq:patch-offsets}$$ The constants below may depend on the fixed band parameters.

Put $\beta=a^{0.2}$ and $b=\beta a$, with $s$ small enough that $\beta<1/100$. For each fixed band parameter $M$, the scales satisfy $$b\ll\ell_j\ll t\ll a.$$ Here each ratio of consecutive scales tends to zero uniformly in $j$, also when $r/s\to0$, by <a href="#eq:packet-scale-ratios" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:packet-scale-ratios">[eq:packet-scale-ratios]</a>. The fine width $b$ makes the semiclassical errors small; the broader master width $t$ absorbs both this smearing and the observation displacement $\ell_j$, while $t/a\to0$ permits a local field comparison. The patch construction below has three outputs. Its energy comparison controls the Coulomb discrepancy and a negative-field penalty. The former, together with noisy-array matching, recovers the conditional density; the latter permits the high-density implication to pass to expectation. Finally, the two conditional-expectation identities recover the mean potential. We establish these transfers in that order.

<a id="eq:patch-counts"></a>
Choose a square cut, using the sine and cosine of a smooth angle, that is full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported out in $B(y,t_0+b)$, and has gradients bounded by $C/b$, where $5a\leq t_0\leq6a$ is selected below. Retain the out particles satisfying $|x_i-y|<t_0-7b$, and call the other out particles deleted.
