Let $n_o$ denote the total number of out particles and $n_{\mathrm{del}}$ the number of deleted particles; thus the retained count is $n_o-n_{\mathrm{del}}$. Then $t_0$ may be chosen so that $$\mathbb E_Q n_o^2\leq C_Ma^{-6},\qquad
 \mathbb E_Q n_{\mathrm{del}}^2\leq C_M\beta a^{-6}.
 \label{eq:patch-counts}$$ For every candidate $t_0$, the total out count is bounded by the raw count in $B(y,7a)$, which lies in a fixed annulus about the nucleus with radius comparable to $a$. The annular count estimate <a href="#eq:annular-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:annular-count">[eq:annular-count]</a>, with <a href="#eq:patch-offsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-offsets">[eq:patch-offsets]</a>, bounds the expected square of this encompassing count by $C_Ma^{-6}$, uniformly in $t_0$. The deleted particles lie in the raw strip $t_0-7b\leq|x_i-y|\leq t_0+b$. Integrating its squared count over $t_0\in[5a,6a]$, each ordered pair contributes for a set of lengths at most $8b$. Averaging selects $t_0$ for the second bound in <a href="#eq:patch-counts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-counts">[eq:patch-counts]</a>, while preserving the first bound.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
###### The fresh conditional core.

In the cut marginal of the tilted state, let $X$ record the out labels, positions, and spins. Slice only on these fresh cut data, not additionally on the noisy arrays. Denote the normalized core slice’s density by $\rho_X^c$ and its energy, including the price per core electron, by $e_X^\lambda$. Then $$e_X^\lambda\geq P(\lambda),\qquad
 \Phi=V-\lambda-K*\rho_X^c.$$ The localization products preserve antisymmetry within each group, and the normalized core slices are in their sector form domains for almost every datum (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)].

<a id="eq:patch-field-envelope"></a>
There is a nonnegative random variable $F=F(X)$ such that $$\Phi\leq F\text{ on }B(y,7a),\qquad
 \norm{F}_{L^2(Q)}\leq C_Ma^{-4}.
 \label{eq:patch-field-envelope}$$ For this, condition the unshifted field obtained by subtracting only electrons outside $B(y,20a)$; all those electrons belong to the core. This field is harmonic on $B(y,10a)$ and bounds $\Phi$ above there. At $z\in B(y,10a)$, the inclusion $B(y,20a)\subset B(z,Aa_z)$ bounds it above by $\mathbb E_Q[J_z\mid X]$. The cut satisfies <a href="#lem:local-field-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-field-input">4</a> uniformly: a bounded number of comparable cells covers its out support, its gradient coefficients obey $D_z^2\leq C\beta^{-2}$, and $a_zm_z\geq ca^{-2}$, so that $D_z^2/(a_zm_z)\leq Ca^{1.6}\leq C$. That lemma bounds the positive part in $L^2(Q)$ at each target $z$. Submeans on balls of radius $a$, followed by Minkowski’s integral inequality, give the positive supremum bound in <a href="#eq:patch-field-envelope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-envelope">[eq:patch-field-envelope]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
###### The patch minimizer.

<a id="eq:patch-euler"></a>
On $\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$ minimize $$\mathcal E_\Phi(f)=T\int f^{5/3}-\int\Phi f+D(f),\qquad f\geq0,
 \quad\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}f\subset\overline\Omega.$$ The field $\Phi$ is bounded and harmonic on a neighborhood of $\overline\Omega$: the core is excluded from $B(y,t_0)$, and the nucleus is far outside this patch. Coercivity in $L^{5/3}(\Omega)$, weak lower semicontinuity, and strict convexity give a unique minimizer $\rho=\rho_X$. Its Euler equation is $$W=\Phi-K*\rho,\qquad \rho=kW_+^{3/2}\text{ on }\Omega.
 \label{eq:patch-euler}$$ Nonnegative variations give the one-sided condition where $\rho=0$; variations of either sign on positive density give the equality. Comparison with zero and $D(\rho)\geq(\int\rho)^2/(24a)$ show that <a id="eq:patch-mass-bound"></a>$$\int\rho\leq CaF.
 \label{eq:patch-mass-bound}$$ These minimizers can be chosen measurably in $X$. One way to see this is to note their continuous dependence in $L^{5/3}$ on a field in $L^{5/2}(\Omega)$: coercivity bounds a converging sequence of minimizers; weak limits minimize the limiting functional by lower semicontinuity and comparison with its minimizer; uniqueness identifies the limit, and convergence of the minimum values forces convergence of the $L^{5/3}$ norms. Uniform convexity gives strong convergence. The Coulomb form is a continuous positive quadratic form on $L^{5/3}(\Omega)$, as used in this argument.

<a id="eq:patch-comparison"></a>
Let $\vartheta_b$ be the radial probability kernel from (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)], obtained by averaging a cube kernel over translations and rotations. It is bounded by $b^{-3}$ and supported in $B(0,\sqrt3b)$. Define $$\sigma=\sum_{i\ \mathrm{retained}}\vartheta_b(\,\cdot-x_i).$$ Every such smear lies in $\Omega$ by the $7b$ retention margin. We claim $$\mathbb E_Q\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\int W_-\sigma\right]
       \leq C_Ma^{-7+.02},\qquad
 \int_{B(y,4a)}\rho_{\mathrm{tilt}}^{5/3}\leq C_Ma^{-7},
 \label{eq:patch-comparison}$$ where $\rho_{\mathrm{tilt}}$ is the ordinary one-particle density of the tilted state. In particular, it is not the conditional patch density $\rho$.

<a id="pm-h-18"></a>
###### Lower and upper energy comparisons.

<a id="eq:priced-localization-identity"></a>
Write $(o,c)$ for the fresh square cut and, for each out-label set $I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}$, set $$\Psi_I=\prod_{i\in I}o(x_i)\prod_{i\notin I}c(x_i)\Psi_{A'},
 \qquad
 T_o=\frac12\sum_{I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}}\sum_{i\in I}
                    \norm{\nabla_i\Psi_I}_2^2.$$ Thus $T_o$ is the total out kinetic energy in the localized state. Adding price to the exact localization identity of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] gives $$\langle\Psi_{A'},H_{Z,n}\Psi_{A'}\rangle+\lambda n
       +\operatorname{err}_{\mathrm{loc}}
 =\mathbb E_Qe_X^\lambda+T_o
       -\mathbb E_Q\sum_{i\ \mathrm{out}}\Phi(x_i)
       +\mathbb E_Q\sum_{i<l\ \mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_l),
 \label{eq:priced-localization-identity}$$ with $0\leq\operatorname{err}_{\mathrm{loc}}\leq C_Mb^{-2}a^{-3}$. The price has been included in the core energy and in $\Phi$; there is no localization error associated with it. (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] allows any measurably selected retained subset and gives <a id="eq:patch-lower-comparison"></a>$$\begin{split}
 T_o&\geq\mathbb E_Q\left[T\int\sigma^{5/3}
                              -Cb^{-2}(n_o^{4/3}+n_o)\right],\\
 \sum_{i<l\ \mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_l)&\geq D(\sigma)-Cn_o/b.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:patch-lower-comparison}$$ The leading kinetic coefficient here is exactly $T$, for two spin states and kinetic energy $-\Delta/2$.

<a id="eq:patch-upper-comparison"></a>
For almost every fixed slice, the corresponding priced upper comparison is $$P(\lambda)\leq e_X^\lambda+T\int\rho^{5/3}
                   +Cb^{-2}\int\rho-\int\Phi\rho+D(\rho).
 \label{eq:patch-upper-comparison}$$ The coherent-packet construction is standard; see (Solovej 2003, Lemma 8.2) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[Solovej2003](#ref-Solovej2003)]. We spell out why the insertion proof of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] also proves this priced version. Integrate the rank-one matrices of the orbitals $g_b(x-z)e^{ip\cdot x}$, for both spins, over $|p|\leq(3\pi^2\rho(z))^{1/3}$ with measure $dz\,dp/(2\pi)^3$. The resulting one-body matrix $\gamma$ satisfies $$0\leq\gamma\leq1,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\gamma=\int\rho,\qquad
 \rho_\gamma=g_b^2*\rho,$$ by Plancherel and momentum-ball integration. Its kinetic trace is $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(-\Delta/2)\gamma=T\int\rho^{5/3}+Cb^{-2}\int\rho.$$ First truncate its spectral decomposition to finitely many eigenvectors. Occupy these independently with probabilities given by the eigenvalues. Every realization is a finite Slater determinant, with an integer number of electrons, and its mean repulsion is at most the direct energy of the truncated density by the direct-minus-exchange identity. Its support is in the packet region, which lies strictly inside $B(y,t_0)$ and is separated from the core and nucleus. It may therefore be wedged with the core. Every resulting state has shifted energy at least $P(\lambda)$, and the mean price paid by the inserted electrons is $\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\gamma$.

Positive spectral truncations have increasing densities and convergent traces. The potentials are bounded on the packet region for each fixed slice, so all one-body terms converge; direct energies converge by monotone convergence. Radial smearing does not increase $D$, and harmonic means replace $\int\Phi(g_b^2*\rho)$ by $\int\Phi\rho$, including the constant price term. Averaging the integer-sector trials and passing to the limit proves <a href="#eq:patch-upper-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-upper-comparison">[eq:patch-upper-comparison]</a>. This reasoning requires neither an integer value of $\int\rho$ nor a measurable choice of a trial determinant as the slice varies.

<a id="eq:patch-error-budget"></a>
For retained particles, harmonic means likewise give $\sum_{i\ \mathrm{retained}}\Phi(x_i)=\int\Phi\sigma$. The deleted attractions cost at most $Fn_{\mathrm{del}}$. Subtract <a href="#eq:patch-upper-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-upper-comparison">[eq:patch-upper-comparison]</a> in <a href="#eq:priced-localization-identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:priced-localization-identity">[eq:priced-localization-identity]</a>, use [eq:shifted-tilt-cost,eq:patch-lower-comparison] [eq:shifted-tilt-cost](#eq:shifted-tilt-cost), [eq:patch-lower-comparison](#eq:patch-lower-comparison), and expand the functional about its minimizer. Convexity of the kinetic term leaves $D(\sigma-\rho)$ and the linear term $$\int\left(\frac{5T}{3}\rho^{2/3}-W\right)(\sigma-\rho)
                 =\int W_-\sigma.$$ The equality uses <a href="#eq:patch-euler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-euler">[eq:patch-euler]</a>: where $\rho>0$ the coefficient vanishes, and where $\rho=0$ it equals $W_-$. By [eq:patch-counts,eq:patch-field-envelope,eq:patch-mass-bound] [eq:patch-counts](#eq:patch-counts), [eq:patch-field-envelope](#eq:patch-field-envelope), [eq:patch-mass-bound](#eq:patch-mass-bound), the resulting bound is $$\mathbb E_Q\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\int W_-\sigma\right]
 \leq\delta'+C_M\left(
       \sqrt\beta\,a^{-7}+b^{-1}a^{-3}
                       +b^{-2}(a^{-4}+a^{-3})\right).
 \label{eq:patch-error-budget}$$ All terms are integrable: for example, $\int\rho\leq CaF$ and $\mathcal E_\Phi(\rho)\leq0$ bound both its kinetic and direct terms by $CaF^2$ in expectation. The exact cut identity also has integrable absolute Coulomb terms by form-domain admissibility.

Since $b=a^{1.2}$, the errors in <a href="#eq:patch-error-budget" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-error-budget">[eq:patch-error-budget]</a>, divided by $a^{-7}$, are bounded by $$C_M\left(a^{4.98}\log^5(e/a)+a^{.1}
                          +a^{2.8}+a^{.6}+a^{1.6}\right).$$ This proves the first part of <a href="#eq:patch-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-comparison">[eq:patch-comparison]</a>. For the second, use $e_X^\lambda\geq P(\lambda)$ directly in <a href="#eq:priced-localization-identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:priced-localization-identity">[eq:priced-localization-identity]</a>. Dropping the nonnegative out repulsion and using $\mathbb E_QFn_o\leq C_Ma^{-7}$ gives $T_o\leq C_Ma^{-7}$. The coarse kinetic-density inequality of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)], on the full-out ball containing $B(y,4a)$, proves the remaining assertion.

<a id="pm-h-19"></a>
#### Recovering the conditional density from the patch

<a id="eq:patch-field-oscillation"></a>
By <a href="#eq:patch-euler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-euler">[eq:patch-euler]</a>, the patch field satisfies $\Delta W=4\pi kW_+^{3/2}$ on $B(y,4a)$. We will use the bounds $$W\leq Ca^{-4}\text{ on }B(y,3a),\qquad
 \sup_{B(y,2t)}|W-W(y)|
                       \leq C\tau\bigl(a^{-4}+W(y)_-\bigr).
 \label{eq:patch-field-oscillation}$$ For the first, compare on the ball of radius $R=4a$ with $$U(x)=\frac{CR^4}{(R^2-|x-y|^2)^4}.$$ For large universal $C$, $\Delta U\leq4\pi kU^{3/2}$; it diverges at the boundary. Testing with the positive part of $W-U$ proves the bound. For the second, the Coulomb potential of $\rho\mathbf 1_{B(y,2a)}$ is $O(a^{-4})$ with gradient $O(a^{-5})$ on that ball, by the first bound and direct integration of the Coulomb kernel. Adding this potential to $W$ leaves a harmonic function bounded above by $Ca^{-4}$. Apply the interior gradient estimate to its nonnegative harmonic distance from this upper bound. Its center value is at most $Ca^{-4}+W(y)_-$, which proves the oscillation estimate. These comparisons are classical or weak positive-part tests: compact $L^{5/3}$ densities have continuous potentials, so $W$ is continuous.

<a id="eq:noisy-array-sandwich"></a>
Define the local averaging operator $$\mathcal R f(y)=\int\mathcal K_z(y)f(z)\,dz.$$ On every compatible path under $Q$, including its fresh cut labels, $$|\mathcal R\sigma(y)-\mu_j(y)|
        \leq C_Mr^{-3}(\ell_j+b)t^{-4}=o(a^{-6}).
 \label{eq:noisy-array-sandwich}$$ Here is the reason this comparison uses the original observation posterior correctly. Match any compatible raw configuration to the $j$th observed multiset. Each matched displacement is at most $\sqrt3\ell_j$, so the raw kernel sum is within $C_Mr^{-3}\ell_jt^{-4}$ of the sum evaluated at that multiset. The same is true of its posterior average $\mu_j$ at these data. Only $C_Mr^{-3}$ entries can contribute, by $\mathcal C_j$. All relevant raw centers lie in the fully retained region of the fresh cut, since $t/a\to0$, $b/a\to0$, and $\ell_j/a\to0$. Replacing each such center by its fine smear changes the sum by at most $C_Mr^{-3}bt^{-4}$. This proves <a href="#eq:noisy-array-sandwich" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:noisy-array-sandwich">[eq:noisy-array-sandwich]</a> without identifying the two posterior laws. The ratios of these errors to $a^{-6}$ are bounded by $$C_M\bigl(a^{.01-4w}+a^{.2-4w}\bigr)=o(1).$$

Let $$G=\{D(\sigma-\rho)\leq a^{-7+.01}\}.$$ By <a href="#eq:patch-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-comparison">[eq:patch-comparison]</a>, $Q(G^c)\leq C_Ma^{.01}$. The function $z\mapsto\mathcal K_z(y)$ is a compactly supported Lipschitz test with gradient norm at most $Ct^{-5/2}$. Coulomb duality (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] therefore gives, on $G$, <a id="eq:local-coulomb-duality"></a>$$|\mathcal R\rho(y)-\mathcal R\sigma(y)|
              \leq Ca^{-7/2+.005}t^{-5/2}=o(a^{-6}).
 \label{eq:local-coulomb-duality}$$ Indeed the relative gain is at least $a^{.005-(5/2)w}$. All the small errors here are uniform for fixed parameters along <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, including scales with arbitrarily small $r/s$.

<a id="eq:high-patch-field"></a>
<a id="eq:low-patch-field"></a>
On $G$, the high-density antecedent in <a href="#eq:fixed-point-failures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:fixed-point-failures">[eq:fixed-point-failures]</a> forces $$W(y)\geq(h-\xi/16)d_y^{-4}.
 \label{eq:high-patch-field}$$ Otherwise <a href="#eq:patch-field-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-oscillation">[eq:patch-field-oscillation]</a> would imply $W(z)\leq(h-\xi/32)d_y^{-4}$ on the support of the averaging kernel. This remains true for arbitrarily negative $W(y)$: its upper estimate contains the nonpositive term $-(1-C\tau)W(y)_-$. The Euler relation, <a href="#eq:packet-approximate-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:packet-approximate-mass">[eq:packet-approximate-mass]</a>, and [eq:noisy-array-sandwich,eq:local-coulomb-duality] [eq:noisy-array-sandwich](#eq:noisy-array-sandwich), [eq:local-coulomb-duality](#eq:local-coulomb-duality) would then contradict the high-density antecedent. In the same way the low-density antecedent forces, on $G$, $$W(y)\leq(h+\xi/16)d_y^{-4}.
 \label{eq:low-patch-field}$$ The upper bound in <a href="#eq:patch-field-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-oscillation">[eq:patch-field-oscillation]</a> lets <a href="#eq:low-patch-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:low-patch-field">[eq:low-patch-field]</a> pass to expectation with an $o(a^{-4})$ error.

The high case needs a bound on the negative field on $G^c$. The high antecedent and <a href="#eq:noisy-array-sandwich" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:noisy-array-sandwich">[eq:noisy-array-sandwich]</a> hold on all paths under $Q$, and give $$\int_{B(y,2t)}\sigma\geq ca^{-6}t^3.$$ Using <a href="#eq:patch-field-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-oscillation">[eq:patch-field-oscillation]</a> on this ball and absorbing $C\tau W(y)_-$ yields <a id="eq:negative-patch-control"></a>$$W(y)_-\leq C(a^{-6}t^3)^{-1}\int W_-\sigma+C\tau a^{-4}.
 \label{eq:negative-patch-control}$$ Consequently <a href="#eq:patch-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-comparison">[eq:patch-comparison]</a> implies $$\mathbb E_Q[\mathbf 1_{G^c}W(y)_-]=o(a^{-4}),$$ since the ratio of its bound to $a^{-4}$ is at most $C_Ma^{.02}\tau^{-3}+C\tau
 \leq C_Ma^{.02-3w}+Cs^w=o(1)$. Thus <a href="#eq:high-patch-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:high-patch-field">[eq:high-patch-field]</a> also passes to expectation with an $o(a^{-4})$ error. This step explicitly controls the negative part; no lower cap on the conditional field is assumed.

<a id="pm-h-20"></a>
#### Matching the potentials of the two conditional laws

<a id="eq:potential-matching"></a>
<a id="eq:two-posterior-potential-identity"></a>
We adapt the averaged field transfer of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] to the present priced fields. The preceding patch estimates supply its required small errors; we give the two-law decomposition and estimates explicitly. We prove $$\left|\mathbb E_Q\bigl(H'_j-W\bigr)(y)\right|=o(a^{-4}).
 \label{eq:potential-matching}$$ Write $P^{\mathrm{raw}}$ and $P^{\mathrm{ms}}$ for the raw and master-smeared electron potentials at $y$, and $P^{\mathrm{ret}},P^{\mathrm{del}}$ for the raw potentials of retained and deleted out particles. Because the event defining $Q$ is in $\mathcal F_j$, the original observation tower gives $\mathbb E_Q(K*\mu_j)(y)=\mathbb E_QP^{\mathrm{ms}}$. The core in $W$ instead uses the fresh-cut tower in the tilted raw law. Combining these two identities, and cancelling both nucleus and price, expresses $\mathbb E_Q(H'_j-W)(y)$ as the expectation of $$(P^{\mathrm{raw}}-P^{\mathrm{ms}})-P^{\mathrm{del}}
    -(P^{\mathrm{ret}}-K*\sigma)(y)+K*(\rho-\sigma)(y).
 \label{eq:two-posterior-potential-identity}$$

<a id="eq:raw-short-range-potential"></a>
By Hölder’s inequality and the ordinary density bound in <a href="#eq:patch-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-comparison">[eq:patch-comparison]</a>, for $0<u\leq4a$ the expected raw potential from $B(y,u)$ is at most $$\int_{B(y,u)}\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{tilt}}(z)}{|z-y|}\,dz
                  \leq C_Ma^{-4}(u/a)^{1/5}.
 \label{eq:raw-short-range-potential}$$ Radial smearing never increases the potential of a point charge and leaves it unchanged outside the smear radius. The master-smearing difference in <a href="#eq:two-posterior-potential-identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:two-posterior-potential-identity">[eq:two-posterior-potential-identity]</a> can therefore come only from centers within $2t$ of $y$; the fine-smearing difference can come only from centers within $\sqrt3b$. bounds both by $o(a^{-4})$. Deleted centers have distance comparable to $a$, so <a href="#eq:patch-counts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-counts">[eq:patch-counts]</a> gives $\mathbb E_QP^{\mathrm{del}}\leq C_Ma^{-4}\sqrt\beta$.

For the remaining signed potential, truncate the kernel at height $t^{-1}$. The test $z\mapsto\min(|z-y|^{-1},t^{-1})$ has homogeneous Sobolev gradient norm $O(t^{-1/2})$. Coulomb duality, or its compactly supported approximation, and <a href="#eq:patch-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-comparison">[eq:patch-comparison]</a> give expected absolute truncated error at most $$C_Mt^{-1/2}a^{-7/2+.01}=o(a^{-4});$$ the relative gain is $a^{.01}\tau^{-1/2}\leq C_Ma^{.01-w/2}$. The truncation loss for $\rho$ is at most $Ca^{-6}t^2$ by <a href="#eq:patch-field-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-oscillation">[eq:patch-field-oscillation]</a>. For $\sigma$, radial smearing and <a href="#eq:raw-short-range-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:raw-short-range-potential">[eq:raw-short-range-potential]</a> bound it by $$C_Ma^{-4}\bigl((t+\sqrt3b)/a\bigr)^{1/5}.$$ These also vanish relative to $a^{-4}$ by <a href="#eq:packet-scale-ratios" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:packet-scale-ratios">[eq:packet-scale-ratios]</a>. This proves <a href="#eq:potential-matching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:potential-matching">[eq:potential-matching]</a>.

In the high case, [eq:high-patch-field,eq:negative-patch-control] [eq:high-patch-field](#eq:high-patch-field), [eq:negative-patch-control](#eq:negative-patch-control) give $\mathbb E_QW(y)\geq(h-\xi/16)d_y^{-4}-o(a^{-4})$, whereas the failed comparison in <a href="#eq:fixed-point-failures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:fixed-point-failures">[eq:fixed-point-failures]</a> gives $\mathbb E_QH'_j(y)\leq(h-\xi/4)d_y^{-4}$. [eq:potential-matching](#eq:potential-matching) contradicts these inequalities eventually. The low case follows in the same way from <a href="#eq:low-patch-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:low-patch-field">[eq:low-patch-field]</a> and the upper bound on $W$. We conclude that each fixed-point failure in <a href="#eq:fixed-point-failures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:fixed-point-failures">[eq:fixed-point-failures]</a>, intersected with $\mathcal C_j$, has probability less than $r^{42}$.

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### Spatial and height nets

Take a spatial net of mesh at most $r^2$ in the original band, with $O_M(r^{-3})$ points, and a fixed finite height net in $[h_l/2,2h_h]$ of mesh at most $\xi/64$. Exclude the fixed-point failures at all net pairs, the complement of $\mathcal C_j$, and the enlarged-cap failure. Their total probability is $O_M(r^{39})+O_M(r^{40})$, in particular at most $Cr^{25}$. All these events are $\mathcal F_j$-measurable.

<a id="eq:net-field-variation"></a>
The normalized density $d_y^6\mu_j(y)$ changes by at most $C_Mr^{1-4w}=o(1)$ between a point and its nearest net point, by <a href="#eq:conditional-density-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:conditional-density-bounds">[eq:conditional-density-bounds]</a>. For the field, work on balls of radius a small fixed multiple of $r$ within the enlarged cap band. Adding the Coulomb potential of the local source $\mu_j$ makes $H'_j$ harmonic there and bounds that harmonic function above by $C_Mr^{-4-3w}$, using [eq:conditional-density-bounds,eq:enlarged-field-cap]. The positive-harmonic gradient argument used in <a href="#eq:patch-field-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:patch-field-oscillation">[eq:patch-field-oscillation]</a> shows that $v(y)=d_y^4H'_j(y)$ varies over a displacement $O(r^2)$ by at most $$C_Mr^{1-3w}+C_Mr\,v(y)_-.
 \label{eq:net-field-variation}$$

Suppose a high-density failure with tolerance $\xi$ occurs at $(y,h)$. If $v(y)\geq0$, <a href="#eq:net-field-variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:net-field-variation">[eq:net-field-variation]</a> puts the nearby net value below $h-3\xi/4$ for small $s$. If $v(y)<0$, the same upper estimate is bounded by $(1-C_Mr)v(y)+C_Mr^{1-3w}$, and gives the identical conclusion since $h\geq h_l>16\xi$. Choose a height-net value between $h-\xi/4$ and $h-\xi/8$. The small normalized-density variation preserves the strict high antecedent at this lower height, while the field value violates its comparison with tolerance $\xi/4$. This is an excluded net failure.

A low-density failure has $v(y)>h+\xi>0$. Its nearest net value is above $h+3\xi/4$ by <a href="#eq:net-field-variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:net-field-variation">[eq:net-field-variation]</a>. A height-net value between $h+\xi/8$ and $h+\xi/4$ preserves the low antecedent and again yields an excluded failure with tolerance $\xi/4$. Thus both comparisons hold everywhere on the band for every $h_l\leq h\leq h_h$. Together with <a href="#eq:enlarged-field-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:enlarged-field-cap">[eq:enlarged-field-cap]</a>, this proves <a href="#prop:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse-comparison">9</a>.

<a id="sec:barriers"></a>
### Continuing a lower barrier

The local density–field comparison in <a href="#prop:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse-comparison">9</a> does not by itself exclude a limiting field which is too small near the nucleus. We now carry a positive barrier from the nuclear scale to an arbitrary intermediate scale. The construction keeps the actual conditional density inside the current radius and uses the Thomas–Fermi reaction outside it. Rare failures of the local comparison are retained as an explicit, small source error. This adapts the intermediate-subsolution construction of (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 11.2) <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)], using the positive-price comparison proved here to allow the growing offset $D_0=O(s^{-7})$.

<a id="eq:barrier-gap"></a>
Write $d=|y|$ and put <a id="eq:barrier-function"></a>$$\label{eq:barrier-function}
 f(t)=4\pi k(t_+)^{3/2},\qquad
 b_r(y)=B d^{-4}\left(1-\frac r{8d}\right)\quad(d\ge r),$$ where $B>0$ will be fixed below. Direct differentiation gives $$\Delta b_r=Bd^{-6}\left(12-\frac{20r}{8d}\right)
       \ge \frac{19}{2}Bd^{-6}.$$ Thus $\Delta b_r\ge f(b_r)$ whenever $4\pi k\sqrt B\le19/2$. If $R=2r$, then $$\label{eq:barrier-gap}
 b_r-b_R\ge\gamma R^{-4}\quad(R\le d\le1.1R),
 \qquad \gamma=\frac{B}{16(1.1)^5}>0.$$

<a id="stmt-prop-barrier-invariant"></a>
<div id="prop:barrier-invariant" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:barrier-invariant"></a>
<a id="eq:barrier-error-mass"></a>
**Proposition 10** (Conditional barrier invariant). *There are fixed positive constants $\varepsilon,B,C_{\mathrm{inv}}$ and $M>2$ with the following property. For every sufficiently far system in <a href="#eq:scaling-regime" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:scaling-regime">[eq:scaling-regime]</a>, use the observations and kernels of [eq:observation-scales,eq:master-kernel] [eq:observation-scales](#eq:observation-scales), [eq:master-kernel](#eq:master-kernel) with this $\varepsilon$. For every $0\le j\le J_*$ there exist $\mathcal F_j$-measurable functions $u_j,p_j$ such that, with $r=r_j$, $$\label{eq:barrier-invariant}
 \begin{aligned}
 \Delta u_j&\ge-4\pi\nu
       +4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{d<r\}}\mu_j-4\pi p_j
       +\mathbf 1_{\{d\ge r\}}f(u_j),\\
 b_r\le u_j&\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\quad(d\ge r),\\
 u_j&=b_r\quad(d>Mr).
 \end{aligned}$$ The first inequality holds in distributions on $\mathbb R^3$. For each fixed system, $u_j-V$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz, with deterministic bounds on compact sets; $p_j$ is a nonnegative bounded density supported in $\{d\le r_j\}$, also with a deterministic bound. Its expected total mass satisfies $$\label{eq:barrier-error-mass}
 \sup_{0\le j\le J_*}\mathbb E\int p_j
 \le C\bigl(r_0^{32}+s^{19/2-3w}\bigr)=o(1).$$ The deterministic fixed-system regularity bounds need not be uniform in $Z$; the constants in <a href="#eq:barrier-error-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:barrier-error-mass">[eq:barrier-error-mass]</a> are uniform.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-barrier-invariant"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-barrier-invariant</summary>

*Proof.* We use the weak maximum rule in (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. In its nonsingular form, if finitely many continuous locally $H^1$ functions satisfy $$\Delta v_i\ge g(y)+F(y,v_i),$$ where $g$ is locally bounded and $F$ is measurable in $y$, continuous in its second variable, and locally bounded on bounded ranges, then their maximum satisfies the same inequality with its own value in the reaction. The lemma also allows a common singular term $-4\pi\nu$ when all branches have continuous locally $H^1$ offsets from $V$ and the reaction vanishes near the origin. These are the precise forms used below.

<a id="pm-h-23"></a>
###### Initialization.

<a id="eq:initial-potential"></a>
For sufficiently small fixed $\varepsilon$, the initial conditional density satisfies, outside an event of probability at most $Cr_0^{35}$, $$\label{eq:initial-potential}
 P_0(y):=K*(\mathbf 1_{\{d<r_0\}}\mu_0)(y)
       \le0.1\,Z/r_0
       \quad(r_0\le |y|\le1.1r_0).$$ To prove this, fix $y$ in the indicated annulus and consider the $\mathcal F_0$-event $A_y=\{P_0(y)>0.05Z/r_0\}$. If its probability is greater than $r_0^{40}$, <a href="#lem:event-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:event-tilt">8</a> realizes its raw conditional marginal as a normalized state in sector $n$ with unshifted offset at most $$D_0+Cr_0^{-2.02}\log^5(e/r_0^{40}).$$ By <a href="#eq:rough-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:rough-deficit">[eq:rough-deficit]</a>, $D_0\le Cs^{-7}$. Since $r_0/s\to0$, the displayed offset is at most $Z^{7/3}$ eventually, with $\varepsilon$ fixed. The global density estimate of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] applies: the state is normalized and antisymmetric and $n\le3Z$, so its ordinary density $\rho_{A_y}$ obeys $\int\rho_{A_y}^{5/3}\le CZ^{7/3}$.

Only packet centers in $|x|<2r_0$ can contribute to the truncated density defining $P_0$. The potential of a truncated packet is at most that of the full packet, and radial smearing bounds the latter by $K(y-x)$. The observation tower and Hölder’s inequality give $$\mathbb E[P_0(y)\mid A_y]
 \le\int_{|x|<2r_0}\frac{\rho_{A_y}(x)}{|y-x|}\,dx
 \le CZ^{7/5}r_0^{1/5}
 =C\varepsilon^{6/5}Z/r_0.$$ Choose $\varepsilon$ so small that the last coefficient is less than $0.05$. This contradicts the event definition. Hence $\mathbb P(A_y)\le r_0^{40}$.

The minimum packet width is $c_1r_0^{1+w}$. Integrating $|y-z|^{-2}$ against each bounded packet yields $$\norm{\nabla P_0}_\infty\le CZr_0^{-2-2w}.$$ A spatial net of spacing at most $r_0^2$ in the annulus has $O(r_0^{-3})$ points. Its union failure probability is $O(r_0^{37})$, and its interpolation error divided by $Z/r_0$ is $O(r_0^{1-2w})$. This proves <a href="#eq:initial-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:initial-potential">[eq:initial-potential]</a>, with the weaker exponent stated there. The good event is measurable because $P_0$ is continuous in space.

Let $G_0$ be this good event and define $$\mu^{\mathrm{in}}=\mathbf 1_{G_0}\mathbf 1_{\{d<r_0\}}\mu_0,
 \qquad p_0=\mathbf 1_{G_0^c}\mathbf 1_{\{d<r_0\}}\mu_0.$$ On $d<2r_0$ consider the branch $$v=V-K*\mu^{\mathrm{in}}-0.7Z/r_0
                +C_3(Z/r_0)^{3/2}d^2.$$ Fix $C_3$ so that $6C_3\ge4\pi k\,2^{3/2}$ and decrease $\varepsilon$ if necessary so that $4C_3\varepsilon^{3/2}\le0.05$. Since $Zr_0^3=\varepsilon^3$, the quadratic term is at most $0.05Z/r_0$ on this ball. On $r_0\le d<2r_0$ we have $v\le2Z/r_0$, and therefore $$\Delta v=-4\pi\nu+4\pi\mu^{\mathrm{in}}
                   +6C_3(Z/r_0)^{3/2},
 \qquad 6C_3(Z/r_0)^{3/2}\ge f(v).$$ On the lower overlap $r_0\le d<1.06r_0$, $$v\ge(1/1.06-0.1-0.7)Z/r_0>0.14Z/r_0;$$ on $G_0^c$ the same bound follows with the $0.1$ term omitted. On $1.9r_0<d<2r_0$, $$v\le(1/1.9-0.7+0.05)Z/r_0<0.$$ Fix $B$ satisfying the subsolution condition above and $0<B\le0.1\varepsilon^3$. Define $u_0$ on an open cover by $$u_0=
 \begin{cases}
 v,&d<1.06r_0,\\
 \max(v,b_{r_0}),&r_0<d<2r_0,\\
 b_{r_0},&d>1.9r_0.
 \end{cases}$$ The definitions agree on overlaps. Near $d=r_0$ the branch $v$ alone is active and supplies both required source inequalities. Elsewhere the weak maximum rule proves <a href="#eq:barrier-invariant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:barrier-invariant">[eq:barrier-invariant]</a>. The exterior cap holds for $C_{\mathrm{inv}}\ge\max(B,32\varepsilon^3)$, and $$\mathbb E\int p_0\le n\mathbb P(G_0^c)\le Cr_0^{32}.$$

<a id="pm-h-24"></a>
###### Choice of the remaining constants.

<a id="eq:barrier-parameters"></a>
Increase $C_{\mathrm{inv}}$ to exceed the universal cap $C_{\mathrm{cap}}$ in <a href="#prop:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse-comparison">9</a>. Choose $$0<\lambda_2<\lambda_1<\gamma/2,
 \qquad M>2,\qquad C_{\mathrm{inv}}M^{-4}<\lambda_2.$$ The constants $\lambda_1,\lambda_2$ are barrier shifts, distinct from the electron price $\lambda=s^{-4}$. Fix $0<h_l<B/4$ and $h_h>C_{\mathrm{inv}}$, and then choose $e_0,\xi>0$ so small that $$\label{eq:barrier-parameters}
 \begin{gathered}
 2e_0(2M)^4<B/4,\qquad
 16\xi+2e_0<\lambda_1-\lambda_2,\\
 \xi<\min(\lambda_2,h_l/16).
 \end{gathered}$$ All these constants are now fixed. The simultaneous comparison <a href="#prop:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse-comparison">9</a> applies with exactly these parameters along the regime under consideration.

<a id="pm-h-25"></a>
###### One outward step.

<a id="eq:barrier-open-cover"></a>
<a id="eq:barrier-added-error"></a>
Suppose the invariant holds at $r=r_j$, where $j<J_*$, and set $R=2r$ and $u=u_j$. On the good event of <a href="#prop:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inverse-comparison">9</a> for the band $r\le d\le4Mr$, use $$v_1=u-\lambda_1R^{-4},\qquad
 v_2=H'_j-\lambda_2R^{-4}.$$ The two branches have complementary roles. On the gained annulus $r\le d<R$, the old reaction must supply the actual density, so a retained $v_1$ must satisfy $f(u)\ge4\pi\mu_j$. Outside $R$, the field branch already has Laplacian $4\pi\mu_j$, which must instead dominate $f(v_2)$. The opposite inverse implications in <a href="#eq:inverse-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:inverse-comparison">[eq:inverse-comparison]</a>, together with the unequal shifts, give these two inequalities on the neighborhoods used below. On bad data omit $v_2$. Define $\widetilde u$ by $$\label{eq:barrier-open-cover}
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{region}&\widetilde u\\ \hline
 d<1.08R&\max(v_1,v_2)\\
 1.04R<d<2MR&\max(v_1,v_2,b_R)\\
 d>MR&b_R
 \end{array}$$ with the same omission on bad data, and set $$\label{eq:barrier-added-error}
 \widetilde p=p_j+\mathbf 1_{\{\mathrm{bad}\}}\mathbf 1_{\{r\le d<R\}}\mu_j.$$ [fig:barrier-step](#fig:barrier-step) shows the overlapping regions in <a href="#eq:barrier-open-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:barrier-open-cover">[eq:barrier-open-cover]</a> and the source-gain annulus $r\le d<R$. On the lower overlap, <a href="#eq:barrier-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:barrier-gap">[eq:barrier-gap]</a> gives $v_1\ge b_r-\lambda_1R^{-4}>b_R$. On the upper overlap, the caps and $C_{\mathrm{inv}}M^{-4}<\lambda_2$ put both shifted branches below $b_R$. Thus the definitions agree. They give $b_R\le\widetilde u\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}$ on $d\ge R$ and $\widetilde u=b_R$ on $d>MR$; for $R\le d<1.08R$ the lower bound already follows from $v_1$.

<figure id="fig:barrier-step">

<figcaption>The overlapping definitions in one continuation step from $r$ to $R = 2r$. The shifted branches are $v_{1} = u_{j} − λ_{1}R^{−4}$ and $v_{2} = H′_{j} − λ_{2}R^{−4}$; the second branch is omitted on bad data. The source term changes on the shaded annulus $r ≤ d < R$. The barrier gap makes the first two definitions agree on $1.04R < d < 1.08R$, and the caps make the last two agree on $MR < d < 2MR$.</figcaption>
</figure>
